View news five-element analysis
Time
The news report was published on 17 July 2026. The event described is the detection of sustained rabies transmission in Cape fur seals, which is reported as an ongoing development.
Location and population
The event involves Cape fur seals in southern Africa. The affected population is the seal population, and the context includes the broader wildlife and human populations in the region.
Event
The World Organisation for Animal Health (WOAH) reports the detection of sustained rabies transmission in Cape fur seals in southern Africa. This is described as an unprecedented development in rabies ecology.
Scale, impact and risk
The report highlights an unprecedented shift in rabies ecology. It raises important questions for wildlife management and public health, indicating a significant and concerning change in disease dynamics.
Response, status and uncertainty
The report identifies the event as a new development requiring attention. It implies a need for further investigation and highlights the importance of the One Health approach to address the risks to wildlife and human health.

B 常规重要

特哈马县沙斯塔的蝙蝠确诊患有狂犬病。如何保持安全

Rabies confirmed in Shasta, Tehama county bats. How to stay safe
完整新闻中文摘要北加州卫生官员在确诊蝙蝠病例后,向沙斯塔县和特哈马县发出狂犬病警告。在沙斯塔县,一只蝙蝠于 2026 年 4 月检测呈阳性,而特哈马县的两只蝙蝠于 2026 年 7 月检测呈阳性。沙斯塔县在 2025 年或 2026 年迄今没有报告人类病例。2026 年 1 月 1 日至 6 月 4 日全州范围内的数据显示,113 只蝙蝠、16 只臭鼬和一只狗的狂犬病检测呈阳性。官员敦促居民避免生病的动物,为宠物接种疫苗,并报告异常行为。如果不治疗,该病毒几乎总是致命的,但如果在 14 天内进行暴露后治疗,则非常有效。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该新闻报道于 2026 年 7 月 16 日发布。该事件涉及 2026 年 4 月沙斯塔县和 2026 年 7 月特哈马县报告的蝙蝠狂犬病确诊病例,还参考了 2025 年的病例数。
地点与对象
受影响人口包括北加州沙斯塔县和特哈马县的居民。受影响的动物是蝙蝠,特别提到了特哈马县的住宅社区和康宁的伍德森桥州立游乐区。据指出,美国普通民众面临蝙蝠传播狂犬病的风险。
事件
北加州卫生官员已就沙斯塔县(四月)和特哈马县(七月)发现的蝙蝠确诊狂犬病病例发出警告。该事件被归类为这些县内确认的蝙蝠狂犬病爆发。
规模、影响与风险
2025年,沙斯塔县有两只蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性,特哈马县有一只蝙蝠检测呈阳性。2025 年或 2026 年迄今,沙斯塔县未报告人间病例。2026 年 1 月 1 日至 6 月 4 日全州范围内的数据显示,113 只蝙蝠、16 只臭鼬和一只狗的狂犬病检测呈阳性。明确指出,如果不及早治疗,这种风险可能致命。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生机构敦促居民避免生病的动物,为宠物接种疫苗,并报告不寻常的动物行为。接触者追踪和测试正在进行中,并提供了具体的联系电话号码以供动物控制和健康查询。来源是完整的原始报告,但没有具体说明 2026 年 7 月在特哈马县发现的蝙蝠的确切数量。
B routine priority

Rabies confirmed in Shasta, Tehama county bats. How to stay safe

Fetched Original Body
With recent findings of rabies-infected bats in Shasta County in April and Tehama County in July, health officials in Northern California are urging residents to stay away from animals that appear to be ill and to vaccinate household pets against the rare and avoidable but potentially deadly viral disease. Most recently, the Tehama County Health Services Agency announced on Tuesday, July 14 that two bats that tested positive for rabies had been found in residential communities. In Shasta County, one bat tested positive for rabies in April, Shasta County Health and Human Services Agency spokesman Christopher Diamond said. No other information was released, but the agency urged anyone with questions about possible exposure to call officials at 530-225-5591. In 2025, two bats tested positive, the Shasta County agency said. No people contracted rabies in 2025 or so far this year in Shasta County, the agency said. Additional information on rabies can be found on the Rabies and Bite Prevention webpage of the Shasta County HHSA website. Rabies in bats is widespread in Tehama County, according to the health agency said. Bats and other wild animals are also the most common source of human rabies infections in the U.S. In Tehama County in September 2023, an infected fox was found in the Woodson Bridge State Recreation Area in Corning, 22 miles south and southeast of Red Bluff, public health officials said. In 2025, one rabies case, in a bat, was confirmed in Tehama County, said the California Department of Public Health's online database. While it's widely believed that rabid animals are easy to spot because they drool excessively and foam at the mouth, most animals will display these symptoms only in the latter stages of infection, if at all, said Tehama County health officials. "A better way to identify animals that pose a risk is to recognize unusual or abnormal behavior. Rabid animals, wild or domestic, may stagger, appear restless, be aggressive, change the tone of their barks or growls, orappear to be choking," said the agency. Sometimes infected wild animals lose their fear of humans and act friendly, while animals that are usually passive can become fierce and aggressive, health officials said. So be alert and call the closest Animal Control office if you spot an animal exhibiting these kinds of distress, health experts advise. From North State health experts, here is more information about avoiding animals with rabies and steps to take should you encounter one. What is rabies? Rabies is a viral disease that affects mammals. It’s “most often transmitted to humans and other animals from the bite of a rabid animal,” Tehama County Public Health Director Minnie Sagar said. “The rabies virus infects the central nervous system, including the brain." After exposure, the virus has to travel to the brain before symptoms appear, an incubation period that can last for weeks or longer, according to the World Health Organization and the U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. Symptoms of rabies Early symptoms of rabies may be similar to influenza: weakness, fever, headache, and/or a prickly or itchy feeling near the bite. Eventually more serious symptoms appear, including anxiety, confusion, agitation, partial paralysis, delirium, hallucinations, a fear of water and insomnia, according to the CDC. Humans and other mammals may experience the same symptoms, public health branches report. More: Rabies found in Corning rec area in 2023: What to know about the virus Rabies is rare in humans in the U.S. California public health agencies report that fewer than 10 people die from rabies each year in the United States. “Bats are the leading cause of rabies deaths in people. Rabies can spread to people from bats after minor, seemingly unimportant, or unrecognized bites or scratches,” according to the CDC's rabies prevention information web page . No cases of rabies were reported in animals between Jan. 1 and June 4, 2026 in Shasta, Siskiyou, Lassen or Trinity counties, according to data from the California Department of Public Health. Statewide during the period covering Jan. 1 to June 4, 2026, California Public Health officials reported rabies cases in 113 bats, 16 skunks and one dog, according to the website. Rabies can kill if not stopped early If allowed to progress to the point when symptoms appear, rabies is almost "always fatal,” according to the CDC. By then, treatments can only help alleviate the patient’s suffering, not cure them. That's why it’s important to act fast if someone gets bitten, the CDC said. While vaccines, medicines and technologies have long been available to prevent death from rabies, the virus kills an estimated 59,000 people worldwide each year, most from bites from infected dogs. Ninety-five percent of human rabies deaths occur in Africa and Asia, according to WHO . Rabies can usually be avoided A good way to avoid rabies and other diseases is to not touch wildlife and stay away from any animal whose behavior seems strange. Teach children to do the same, Sagar said. If wounded by an animal, wash the wound thoroughly with soap and water and get medical treatment right away, Sagar said. Also vaccinate pets against rabies and don’t allow them to chase wildlife or otherwise expose themselves, according to public health branches. Anti-rabies shots are given where ? Until the 1980s, a person exposed to rabies required a series of painful shots in their stomach. Not anymore, Sagar said. Post-exposure treatment is usually an injection to boost the immune system and four additional injections over a two-week period, all in the arm. That five-shot series “has proven 100% effective if started within 14 days of exposure,” Sagar said. Common side effects of the shots are redness and soreness in the arm, she said. Quick action to take if you've been exposed Any wild mammal (such as a raccoon, skunk, fox, coyote or bat) can have rabies and transmit it to people. Call your local animal regulation service to report potential exposures, to remove a bat, for rabies testing of the bat or other animal or other information: • Unincorporated Shasta County Animal Control: 530-245-6065• City of Redding and Anderson Animal Regulation: 530-241-2550• Shasta Lake Animal Control: 530-275-7480 Michele Chandler covers public safety, reports on major trials happening in Shasta County Superior Court, writes about restaurants and handles whatever else comes up for the Redding Record Searchlight/USA Today Network. Accepts story tips at 530-338-7753 and at mrchandler@gannett.com. Please support our entire newsroom's commitment to public service journalism by subscribing today . This article originally appeared on Redding Record Searchlight: Rabies warnings issued for Tehama, Shasta county residents
View news five-element analysis
Time
The news report was published on July 16, 2026. The event involves confirmed rabies cases in bats reported in Shasta County in April 2026 and Tehama County in July 2026, with a 2025 case count also referenced.
Location and population
The affected population includes residents of Shasta County and Tehama County in Northern California. The affected animals are bats, with specific mentions of residential communities in Tehama County and the Woodson Bridge State Recreation Area in Corning. The general population of the U.S. is noted as being at risk from bat-borne rabies.
Event
Health officials in Northern California have issued warnings regarding confirmed rabies cases in bats found in Shasta County (April) and Tehama County (July). The event is classified as a confirmed outbreak of rabies in bats within these counties.
Scale, impact and risk
In 2025, two bats tested positive for rabies in Shasta County, and one bat tested positive in Tehama County. No human cases were reported in 2025 or so far in 2026 in Shasta County. Statewide data for January 1 to June 4, 2026, shows 113 bats, 16 skunks, and one dog tested positive for rabies. The risk is explicitly stated as potentially deadly if not treated early.
Response, status and uncertainty
Health agencies are urging residents to avoid sick animals, vaccinate pets, and report unusual animal behavior. Contact tracing and testing are ongoing, with specific contact numbers provided for animal control and health inquiries. The source is a full original report, but the exact number of bats found in Tehama County in July 2026 is not specified.

B 常规重要

官员敦促阿什维尔野生动物救援游客接受可能的狂犬病暴露检查 - 福克斯卡罗来纳新闻

Officials urge Asheville wildlife rescue visitors to get checked for possible rabies exposure - FOX Carolina News
完整新闻中文摘要北卡罗来纳州邦库姆县卫生官员敦促前往阿什维尔阿勒尤普野生动物救援和康复中心的游客联系当地卫生部门进行狂犬病风险评估和可能的暴露后预防 (PEP)。该建议针对的是过去六个月内访问过该设施并接触过野生动物的任何人。如果及时护理,人类狂犬病是 100% 可以治愈的。官员强调,获得许可的康复人员必须拥有特定的认证才能处理蝙蝠和浣熊等高风险物种。建议居民避免接近野生动物,如果怀疑接触野生动物,请联系当地卫生部门。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 16 日;该事件涉及过去六个月内前往野生动物救援中心的游客。
地点与对象
北卡罗来纳州邦科姆县,特别是坎德勒的沙山路地区。受影响的人群包括米歇尔·科尔 (Michelle Cole) 或阿勒耶普野生动物救援与康复中心 (Alleyoop Wildlife Rescue and Rehabilitation) 与野生动物接触过的游客。
事件
由于可能接触狂犬病,邦库姆县卫生官员敦促前往阿什维尔 Alleyoop 野生动物救援和康复中心的游客进行狂犬病风险评估和可能的暴露后预防 (PEP)。该活动是一次公共卫生咨询。
规模、影响与风险
所提供的证据中没有报告病例或死亡的规模。该风险被定义为在该设施中接触过野生动物的游客可能接触狂犬病。如果及时治疗,人类狂犬病是 100% 可治愈的。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生官员鼓励风险评估和 PEP。建议居民每周 7 天、每天 24 小时拨打 828-250-5109 联系 Buncombe 县卫生与公众服务部传染病工作人员。来源是福克斯卡罗莱纳新闻的完整报道。

Officials urge Asheville wildlife rescue visitors to get checked for possible rabies exposure - FOX Carolina News

Rabies in humans is 100% treatable if timely care is sought.By Mary Kate Howland Published: Jul. 16, 2026 at 4:59 PM UTCShareAdd Us On GoogleAdd as a preferred source on GoogleBUNCOMBE COUNTY, N.C. (FOX Carolina) - Buncombe County health officials are encouraging anyone who visited a certain wildlife rescue center in Asheville in the last six months to contact their local health department for a rabies risk assessment.Officials said that anyone who visited Michelle Cole or Alleyoop Wildlife Rescue and Rehabilitation in Asheville in the Sand Hill Road area of Candler and had contact with the wildlife there may need rabies post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP), depending on the nature of the exposure.In North Carolina, licensed wildlife rehabilitators must possess a Captivity License for Rehabilitation, an additional rabies vector species certification, and have a complete rabies pre-exposure series to be allowed to handle and rehabilitate animal species that are at high risk of carrying rabies virus, such as bats, raccoons, skunks, foxes and bobcats. In Buncombe County and other counties where the US Department of Agriculture’s Oral Rabies Vaccination program is conducted, the rehabilitation and release of rabies vector species is not authorized except for bats. Unlicensed individuals are not allowed to possess or provide care to wildlife due to safety and public health concerns.Officials encourage residents to do the following:Do not approach, feed, or handle wildlife. If a wild animal or stray pet near your home appears sick or is behaving unusually, or if you find a bat inside your home, contact the North Carolina Wildlife Resources Commission Wildlife Helpline 866-318-2401 and HWI@ncwildlife.gov or your county’s Animal Control.Keep dogs, cats, ferrets, and livestock up to date on rabies vaccinations.Do not feed pets outdoors.Supervise pets when they are outside; a fenced yard provides the best protection.Buncombe County residents who think that they may have been exposed to animals at this facility should contact Buncombe County Health & Human Services Communicable Disease staff 24/7 at 828-250-5109. Residents of other counties should contact the local health department in their county of residence. For more information on rabies prevention and wildlife, residents are encouraged to contact the NC Wildlife Resources Commission at 866-318-2401.Stay with FOX Carolina as we work to learn more.Feel more informed, prepared, and connected with FOX Carolina. 圣劳伦斯县浣熊狂犬病检测呈阳性;今年第八起案件

Raccoon tests positive for rabies in St. Lawrence County; eighth case this year
完整新闻中文摘要经圣劳伦斯县公共卫生部门和纽约州卫生部沃兹沃斯实验室证实,来自纽约州圣劳伦斯县迪卡尔布地区的一只浣熊狂犬病检测呈阳性。这是今年迄今为止该县第八只狂犬病检测呈阳性的动物。卫生官员敦促居民报告动物咬伤事件,为宠物接种疫苗,并避免接触野生动物以防止感染。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布时间:世界标准时间 2026 年 7 月 17 日下午 2:26。据报道,活动日期为 2026 年 7 月 17 日。
地点与对象
美国纽约州劳伦斯县。受影响的动物是来自迪卡尔布地区的浣熊。面临风险的人群包括圣劳伦斯县的居民及其宠物。
事件
迪卡尔布地区的一只浣熊狂犬病检测呈阳性。圣劳伦斯县公共卫生部门证实了这一点。该检测由美国农业部提交,并得到纽约州卫生部沃兹沃斯实验室的确认。这是圣劳伦斯县今年迄今为止第八只狂犬病检测呈阳性的动物。
规模、影响与风险
这是 2026 年圣劳伦斯县确认的第八例狂犬病病例。狂犬病是一种致命病毒,通过受感染动物的唾液或脑组织传播,通常通过咬伤或抓伤传播。未接种疫苗的猫、狗和其他家畜在接触患有狂犬病的野生动物后也可能被感染。卫生官员明确指出了居民及其宠物面临的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
劳伦斯县公共卫生部门敦促居民报告动物咬伤或可能接触狂犬病的情况,让宠物和牲畜及时接种狂犬病疫苗,在户外监督宠物,避免喂养野生动物,并立即清洗伤口。卫生官员建议尽可能安全地收容该动物并报告事件。2026 年免费狂犬病诊所时间表可在圣劳伦斯县网站上找到。

B routine priority

Fox tests positive for rabies in Georgetown County

Fetched Original Body
GEORGETOWN COUNTY, S.C. (WCBD) – On July 16, 2026, a fox in the Georgetown County area near Plantersville Road and N Fraser Street tested positive for the rabies virus, according to the South Carolina Department of Public Health (DPH). “Due to the possibility of the spread of rabies, DPH urges you to use caution when dealing with wild or stray animals,” the department said. “If you believe you, someone you know, or your pets have come in contact with this fox or another animal that potentially has rabies, please call DPH’s Conway office at (843) 915-8801 (Option 7) during normal business hours (8:30 a.m.–5 p.m., Monday–Friday) or the DPH after-hours service number at (888) 847-0902 (Select Option 2),” the department said. DPH says if your pet is ever found with wounds of unknown origin, know that your pet could have been exposed to rabies and contact the Conway office. “This is especially important as rabies has been confirmed in your area. Take any injured pet to a veterinarian so the wounds can be treated and the administration of a rabies vaccination can be discussed,” the department said. Proof of rabies vaccination for pets (cats, dogs, and ferrets) is required by the South Carolina Rabies Control Act. “If your pet does not have a current rabies vaccination, contact a veterinarian as soon as possible for an appointment,” the department said. For more information on rabies or to find a low-cost rabies clinic in South Carolina, click here . Copyright 2026 Nexstar Media, Inc. All rights reserved. It is the first confirmed case in Yellowstone County this year and the third reported statewide in 2026.Watch the story here: Yellowstone County reports confirmed rabies case, officials stress pet vaccinations 1/1 Skip Ad Continue watchingafter the adVisit Advertiser websiteGO TO PAGE Dr. Edie Best, owner and lead veterinarian at Billings Animal Family Hospital, said while rabies cases remain uncommon, the disease is nearly always fatal once symptoms develop, making prevention critical."Rabies is 100% fatal," Best said. "The one vaccination that is absolutely mandatory is the rabies vaccination."Rabies is a viral disease that affects the nervous system and can spread to both animals and humans through bites or scratches from infected animals. Health officials say residents should never approach or handle wild animals, particularly species known to carry the virus, including bats, skunks, raccoons, and foxes.Best said unusual behavior is often the first warning sign."If you see one of those animals out in the daytime or acting unusually, like a bat that's laying on the ground or a skunk that's out in the middle of the day, ... stay away," she said. "That's not normal."While human rabies infections are rare, any potential exposure should be treated as a medical emergency."If you get bit by that animal, that's an emergency room trip immediately," Best said. "The hard part about testing for rabies is the only way to test is to test the brain, so the animal has to be deceased."According to the Montana Department of Livestock, there have been 27 confirmed rabies detections in Montana since July 2025, all involving bats. The Yellowstone County case marks the county's first confirmed detection this year.Best said residents shouldn't panic but should remain aware of their surroundings, especially when spending time outdoors."We've always known it's out there," she said. "It's just a heads up. If you see something that really seems unusual, especially in one of those animals, it's probably not normal." GBHS 将为在疫苗召回后接种的动物提供免费的狂犬病疫苗替代注射 - wvtm

GBHS to provide free replacement rabies shots for animals vaccinated there after vaccine recall - wvtm

中文翻译暂不可用；以下中文显示位置使用已核验英文内容填充。

完整新闻中文摘要阿拉巴马州大伯明翰人道协会 (GBHS) 正在为 2025 年 7 月至 2026 年 4 月期间接受过召回批次狂犬病疫苗的宠物提供免费重新接种诊所。此次召回由制造商 Elanco 发布,影响了 7 个批次,这些疫苗可能会在预期 12 个月保护期结束之前失效。GBHS 强调,接种疫苗的宠物不会立即面临健康风险,但建议重新接种疫苗作为预防措施。诊所从 7 月 21 日开始,持续到 8 月 21 日;业主应检查疫苗接种记录或联系 GBHS 以确认资格。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026年07月18日 下午12:57 CDT。事件日期:2025年7月—2026年4月(接种期);加强针接种门诊自2026年07月21日起运行至2026年08月21日。
地点与对象
美国(全国),位于阿拉巴马州伯明翰的大伯明翰人道协会 (GBHS)。受影响人群:在 GBHS 或其他机构使用召回的狂犬病疫苗批次接种疫苗的宠物。
事件
制造商 Elanco 自愿召回 7 个批次的狂犬病疫苗,因为它们可能在 12 个月的预期保护期结束前失效。GBHS 和其他兽医正在为受影响的动物提供免费的重新疫苗接种诊所。作为预防措施进行报告;没有与召回相关的疫苗失效或狂犬病确诊病例。
规模、影响与风险
所提供的证据中未报告。召回七个疫苗批次(E155948A、E155949A、E158770A、E162767A、E162767C、E167567A、E191877A)。2026 年(截至 7 月)阿拉巴马州报告的狂犬病病例为 27 例,而 2025 年同期为 13 例,但证据并未证实与召回有直接联系。
应对、状态与不确定性
GBHS and Elanco are coordinating free revaccination clinics starting 2026-07-21 through 2026-08-21. Pet owners are advised to check vaccination records or contact GBHS to verify if their pet received an affected dose. No emergency veterinary care is required solely due to the recall. Uncertainty: The evidence does not specify the total number of animals affected or the exact mechanism of reduced vaccine effectiveness.

Updated: 12:57 PM CDT Jul 18, 2026 Editorial Standards i Zoe Blair Multimedia Journalist Riley Conlon Digital Producer Advertisement GBHS to provide free replacement rabies shots for animals vaccinated there after vaccine recall Updated: 12:57 PM CDT Jul 18, 2026 Editorial Standards i Zoe Blair Multimedia Journalist Riley Conlon Digital Producer Some pets vaccinated at the Greater Birmingham Humane Society (GBHS) over the past year may need another rabies shot after a vaccine manufacturer recalled several production batches."It's just not as effective and we want to use the one they want to stand behind," Ivana Sullivan, chief program officer at GBHS, said.The recall applies to pets vaccinated between July 2025 and April 2026. According to GBHS, manufacturer Elanco determined through follow-up testing that seven batches of the vaccine could lose effectiveness before the end of the expected 12-month protection period, even though they met required standards when they were first released. GBHS said the recall does not mean vaccinated pets have rabies or are expected to become ill, but that another dose may be required earlier than expected."This doesn't mean that there's anything bad that's going to happen, or that your animal has rabies or anything like that," Sullivan said. "It just means that there's a possibility that the vaccine that was used is just not as effective as it should be and just to revaccinate.">> YOUR NEIGHBORHOOD: Community coverage from WVTM 13“Protecting the health of pets and the safety of the families who love them is our highest priority,” said Dr. Russell Johnson, Chief Medical Officer of the Greater Birmingham Humane Society. “As soon as we learned of the manufacturer’s recall, we launched a free revaccination program for affected pets. We understand that pet owners may have questions, and our team is committed to making this process as simple, convenient, and transparent as possible.”The recalled vaccine lot numbers are: E155948AE155949AE158770AE162767AE162767CE167567AE191877APet owners can check their vaccination certificates to see whether their pet received one of the affected doses. Those who no longer have their records or are unsure whether their pet is affected can contact GBHS to verify their vaccination history."This is nationwide, this is not just here at the shelter," Sullivan said. "It's something that everybody should call their vet and say, hey, did my dog or my cat get vaccinated with any of the recalled, rabies vaccines? And then everybody can get those handled if they're in that category.">> WVTM 13 ON-THE-GO: Download our app for freeFree revaccination clinics will be offered beginning July 21. According to GBHS, appointments will be available through Aug. 21, and owners do not need to seek emergency veterinary care solely because of the recall. Appointments can be made on the GBS website. BIRMINGHAM, Ala. — Some pets vaccinated at the Greater Birmingham Humane Society (GBHS) over the past year may need another rabies shot after a vaccine manufacturer recalled several production batches. "It's just not as effective and we want to use the one they want to stand behind," Ivana Sullivan, chief program officer at GBHS, said. Advertisement The recall applies to pets vaccinated between July 2025 and April 2026. According to GBHS, manufacturer Elanco determined through follow-up testing that seven batches of the vaccine could lose effectiveness before the end of the expected 12-month protection period, even though they met required standards when they were first released. GBHS said the recall does not mean vaccinated pets have rabies or are expected to become ill, but that another dose may be required earlier than expected. "This doesn't mean that there's anything bad that's going to happen, or that your animal has rabies or anything like that," Sullivan said. "It just means that there's a possibility that the vaccine that was used is just not as effective as it should be and just to revaccinate." >> YOUR NEIGHBORHOOD: Community coverage from WVTM 13 “Protecting the health of pets and the safety of the families who love them is our highest priority,” said Dr. Russell Johnson, Chief Medical Officer of the Greater Birmingham Humane Society. “As soon as we learned of the manufacturer’s recall, we launched a free revaccination program for affected pets. We understand that pet owners may have questions, and our team is committed to making this process as simple, convenient, and transparent as possible.” The recalled vaccine lot numbers are: E155948A E155949A E158770A E162767A E162767C E167567A E191877A Pet owners can check their vaccination certificates to see whether their pet received one of the affected doses. Those who no longer have their records or are unsure whether their pet is affected can contact GBHS to verify their vaccination history. "This is nationwide, this is not just here at the shelter," Sullivan said. "It's something that everybody should call their vet and say, hey, did my dog or my cat get vaccinated with any of the recalled, rabies vaccines? And then everybody can get those handled if they're in that category." >> WVTM 13 ON-THE-GO: Download our app for free Free revaccination clinics will be offered beginning July 21. According to GBHS, appointments will be available through Aug. 21, and owners do not need to seek emergency veterinary care solely because of the recall. Appointments can be made on the GBS website . Time
Publication date: 2026-07-18 12:57 PM CDT. Event date: July 2025–April 2026 (vaccination period); revaccination clinics begin 2026-07-21 and run through 2026-08-21.
Location and population
United States (nationwide), Greater Birmingham Humane Society (GBHS) in Birmingham, Alabama. Affected population: pets vaccinated at GBHS or other facilities using recalled rabies vaccine batches.
Event
Voluntary recall of seven rabies vaccine batches by manufacturer Elanco due to potential loss of effectiveness before the end of the expected 12-month protection period. GBHS and other veterinarians are offering free revaccination clinics for affected animals. Reported as a precautionary measure; no confirmed cases of vaccine failure or rabies linked to the recall.
Scale, impact and risk
Not reported in the supplied evidence. Seven vaccine batches recalled (E155948A, E155949A, E158770A, E162767A, E162767C, E167567A, E191877A). Alabama rabies cases reported as 27 in 2026 (as of July), compared to 13 in the same period in 2025, but no direct link to the recall is established in the evidence.
Response, status and uncertainty
GBHS and Elanco are coordinating free revaccination clinics starting 2026-07-21 through 2026-08-21. Pet owners are advised to check vaccination records or contact GBHS to verify if their pet received an affected dose. No emergency veterinary care is required solely due to the recall. Uncertainty: The evidence does not specify the total number of animals affected or the exact mechanism of reduced vaccine effectiveness.

圣地亚哥动物园野生动物园发现的蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性

Bat found at at San Diego Zoo Safari Park tests positive for rabies
完整新闻中文摘要2026 年 7 月 11 日,圣地亚哥动物园野生动物园发现的一只野生蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性,这是圣地亚哥县今年第 19 起此类病例。死亡蝙蝠于中午 12 点 10 分被收集。在 Oasis on the Rocks 餐厅区发现了蝙蝠,并于 7 月 13 日确诊。该县公共卫生官员敦促任何可能与蝙蝠直接接触过的人立即寻求医疗评估,因为一旦出现症状,狂犬病几乎总是致命的。没有人与人接触的报道。卫生当局强调及时进行接触后预防,并提醒公众不要接触野生动物。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布时间:2026年07月16日 17:36 UTC。事件日期:2026年07月11日 12:10 UTC(蝙蝠被发现并采集)。
地点与对象
美国加利福尼亚州圣地亚哥县;圣地亚哥动物园野生动物园,特别是在岩石绿洲餐厅区。
事件
在圣地亚哥动物园野生动物园收集的一只死去的野生蝙蝠经检测呈狂犬病阳性。圣地亚哥县公共卫生服务部门报告为确诊阳性病例。
规模、影响与风险
1只确诊狂犬病阳性蝙蝠。没有人与人接触的报道。风险:如果症状出现后不及时治疗,狂犬病几乎 100% 致命;及时的暴露后预防非常有效。这是 2026 年圣地亚哥县第 19 只狂犬病检测呈阳性的蝙蝠(2025 年有 21 只)。
应对、状态与不确定性
公共卫生官员正在调查潜在的人类接触情况,并敦促任何直接接触者立即联系卫生服务机构。这只蝙蝠不属于公园的收藏品。没有人间病例报告。官员强调不要接触蝙蝠,如果发生接触就寻求医疗建议。测试于 2026 年 7 月 13 日确认。

Bat found at at San Diego Zoo Safari Park tests positive for rabies

Fetched Original Body
Health Wild Bat Found at Safari Park Tests Positive for Rabies By Cassie N. Saunders , County of San Diego Communications Office Jul. 15, 2026 | 5:36 PM Reading Time: 2 minutes County public health officials are asking anyone who may have come into contact with a bat found Saturday, July 11 at the San Diego Zoo Safari Park to contact them after the animal tested positive for rabies. The deceased bat was found at 12:10 p.m. that day at the Oasis on the Rocks restaurant and was collected by a trained park employee. No human contact with the bat has been reported. If you or someone in your family or group had direct contact with the bat, or you know of someone who may have touched the bat, County Public Health Services urges you to call (619) 692-8499 as soon as possible. Only those who have direct contact with the bat, such as touching or holding the animal, are at risk for rabies. The bat was not one of the park’s collection animals. It was delivered to the County Public Health Laboratory on July 13 where testing confirmed it was positive for rabies. This is the 19th bat to test positive for rabies in San Diego County this year. In 2025, 21 bats tested positive. “Rabies is an extremely serious viral disease, one of the deadliest, with nearly 100% fatality if not caught early enough,” said Dr. Ankita Kadakia, County deputy public health officer. “We are investigating to ensure anyone who came into contact with the bat receives care quickly. Once symptoms start there is no treatment for rabies.” Public health officials remind residents: Do not touch bats or any wildlife. If you have direct contact with a bat, wash the exposed area with soap and seek medical advice. Rabies symptoms can take weeks to months to appear, and once symptoms begin, the disease is almost always fatal. Prompt treatment is highly effective. Rabies remains rare in humans due to prevention efforts. California’s most recent human rabies case occurred in 2024 in a Fresno County resident who was exposed to a bat in Merced County. Find more information about bats and other animal testing for rabies visit the rabies dashboard for additional information rabies view the County’s website. Bats play an important role in our local ecosystem. Many species of bats live in San Diego County, feeding on insects such as mosquitos. Other bat species feed on nectar from plants and pollinate them in the process. You may see bats occasionally around sunset when they are most active. It is important to respect wildlife and give wild animals space to play their role in our natural environment. Related: bat rabies
View news five-element analysis
Time
Published: 2026-07-16 17:36 UTC. Event date: 2026-07-11 12:10 UTC (bat found and collected).
Location and population
San Diego County, California, USA; San Diego Zoo Safari Park, specifically at the Oasis on the Rocks restaurant area.
Event
A deceased wild bat collected at the San Diego Zoo Safari Park tested positive for rabies. Reported by San Diego County Public Health Services as a confirmed positive case.
Scale, impact and risk
1 confirmed rabies-positive bat. No human contact reported. Risk: rabies is nearly 100% fatal if untreated after symptom onset; prompt post-exposure prophylaxis is highly effective. This is the 19th bat to test positive for rabies in San Diego County in 2026 (21 in 2025).
Response, status and uncertainty
Public health officials are investigating potential human exposures and urging anyone with direct contact to contact health services immediately. The bat was not part of the park’s collection. No human cases reported. Officials emphasize not touching bats and seeking medical advice if contact occurs. Testing confirmed on July 13, 2026.

圣地亚哥动物园野生动物园发现的野生蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性 - 10News.com

Wild bat found at San Diego Zoo Safari Park tests positive for rabies - 10News.com
完整新闻中文摘要圣地亚哥县公共卫生官员于 2026 年 7 月 17 日报告,7 月 11 日在圣地亚哥动物园野生动物园发现的一只野生蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性。这只蝙蝠是在 Oasis on the Rocks 餐厅区发现的,由一名训练有素的员工收集,并于 7 月 13 日确认呈阳性。这是 2026 年圣地亚哥县第 19 只狂犬病检测呈阳性的蝙蝠。目前尚未有人类与该蝙蝠接触的报道。卫生当局敦促任何可能接触过蝙蝠的人致电 619-692-8499 进行评估,并指出如果在症状出现后不及时治疗,狂犬病几乎总是致命的。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026-07-17。事件日期:2026-07-11(发现蝙蝠)。检测日期:2026-07-13(检测结果呈阳性)。
地点与对象
美国加利福尼亚州圣地亚哥县。具体地点:圣地亚哥动物园野生动物园(San Diego Zoo Safari Park),位于Oasis on the Rocks餐厅区域。受影响人群:2026-07-11当日到场的访客及工作人员。
事件
在圣地亚哥动物园野生动物园发现的一只野生蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性。由圣地亚哥县公共卫生服务部门报告。现状:确诊蝙蝠狂犬病阳性病例。
规模、影响与风险
1只蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性。没有人类与蝙蝠接触的报道。这是 2026 年圣地亚哥县第 19 只蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性。2025 年,有 21 只蝙蝠检测呈阳性。据描述,狂犬病如果在症状出现后不及时治疗,几乎 100% 致命。
应对、状态与不确定性
圣地亚哥县公共卫生服务部正在调查此事,并呼吁可能在2026-07-11与该蝙蝠有过接触的人士联系卫生当局(电话:619-692-8499)。仅直接接触(触摸或持握)存在狂犬病传播风险。公共卫生官员建议避免接触野生动物,立即清洗任何接触部位,并寻求医疗建议。一旦症状出现,无可用治疗方法。该蝙蝠并非动物园动物收藏的一部分。

Rabies alert: Raccoon tests positive after Snellville dog attack - FOX 5 Atlanta

Fetched Original Body
Rabies alert: Raccoon tests positive after Snellville dog attack FOX 5 Atlanta Digital Team Tue, July 14, 2026 at 12:55 AM UTC The Brief A rabid raccoon attacked three dogs near Tree View Drive in Snellville on July 8, triggering a multiagency public health warning. Public health officials warn Snellville residents to stay away from animals behaving oddly, as the rabies virus is nearly always fatal if untreated. The three dogs involved are undergoing veterinary evaluations and strict rabies post-exposure management. SNELLVILLE, Ga. - A raccoon tested positive for rabies after a dog attack in Snellville, prompting public health officials to issue an immediate warning for residents to avoid animals behaving oddly. Rabid raccoon attack What we know A raccoon tested positive for rabies after it was attacked by three dogs on July 8 near Tree View Drive in Snellville. The three dogs involved are currently undergoing veterinary evaluation and rabies post-exposure management, according to Gwinnett County Animal Welfare and Enforcement and GNR Public Health. Officials advise all pet owners to make sure their pets are up to date on their rabies vaccinations. National Association of State Health Veterinarians guidelines state that unvaccinated dogs and cats exposed to a rabid animal must face a strict four-month quarantine and receive a vaccination one month before release. What is rabies? Dig deeper The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention notes that the rabies virus attacks the central nervous system and is almost always fatal in humans if left untreated. Early symptoms in humans include a fever, headache and general weakness or discomfort. Health officials urge anyone bitten or scratched by a stray or suspected rabid animal to seek immediate medical care and report the exposure. Unusual animal behavior can be reported to the Gwinnett Animal Welfare and Enforcement Bite Office. Public health response What we don't know Officials have not yet confirmed the current health status of the three dogs or whether they were vaccinated against rabies at the time of the confrontation. It remains unknown if any other wild animals or pets in the Snellville area were exposed to the rabid raccoon before the attack occurred. Protect your family What you can do If you are bitten or scratched by a stray or suspected rabid animal, seek medical care immediately and tell your healthcare provider about the exposure. After seeking medical treatment, contact GNR Public Health at 770-339-4260 to speak with the on-call epidemiologist. To report an animal acting strangely and request a pickup, call the Gwinnett Animal Welfare and Enforcement Bite Office at 770-339-3200, ext. 5576. The Source The information in this story was gathered from Gwinnett County Animal Welfare and Enforcement and GNR Public Health, who issued a joint public safety advisory, as well as medical data from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention and quarantine guidelines from the National Association of State Health Veterinarians

一位女士表示,人工智能视觉搜索帮助她意识到自己被蝙蝠咬伤,需要注射狂犬病疫苗

Woman Says AI Visual Search Helped Her Realize She’d Been Bitten by a Bat and Needed a Rabies Shot
完整新闻中文摘要2026年5月,来自利物浦的53岁私人教练劳拉·霍顿在希腊莱斯博斯岛度假时救起了一只落水蝙蝠。蝙蝠飞走后,她使用谷歌镜头识别了这只动物,并被警告可能会感染狂犬病。人工智能工具促使她寻求医疗救助,在那里她被诊断出有咬痕,并开始进行一系列狂犬病暴露后预防注射。霍顿认为人工智能工具救了她的命,并指出她最初并没有感觉到被咬。她希望分享自己的故事能够提高人们对遭遇野生动物风险的认识。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026 年 7 月 15 日。活动日期:2026 年 5 月(希腊莱斯博斯岛度假期间)。
地点与对象
利物浦,英格兰(劳拉·霍顿,53岁私人教练)。希腊(莱斯沃斯岛,公寓大楼游泳池)。
事件
劳拉·霍顿 (Laura Horton) 于 2026 年 5 月救起了一只落水蝙蝠,并对其进行了处理,随后使用 Google Lens AI 识别了蝙蝠的物种和潜在的狂犬病风险。她随后寻求医疗救助并接受狂犬病暴露后预防(PEP)。
规模、影响与风险
一件已确认的人类接触事件。最初并没有感觉到蝙蝠的咬伤。人工智能工具促使立即进行医疗干预,防止潜在的狂犬病感染。文章引用了一般医学建议,即一旦出现症状,狂犬病几乎总是致命的。
应对、状态与不确定性
医疗治疗已开始并完成(在希腊和返回英国后进行 PEP 剂量)。消息来源是西南新闻社通过 PEOPLE 发布的联合报道。蝙蝠的狂犬病确切状况尚未得到证实。

Woman Says AI Visual Search Helped Her Realize She’d Been Bitten by a Bat and Needed a Rabies Shot “It was lucky Google Lens told me to go to hospital — who knows what might have happened?” Laura Horton said By Vanessa Etienne Vanessa Etienne Vanessa Etienne is a Staff Writer for PEOPLE on the Health team. She joined the brand in 2021. People Editorial Guidelines Published on July 15, 2026 04:41PM EDT 1 Comment Laura Horton and the bat she found. Credit : Laura Horton/SWNS NEED TO KNOW Laura Horton used Google Lens to identify a bat she rescued and discovered potential rabies exposureThe AI tool prompted her to seek medical attention after noticing bite marks she hadn’t feltHorton hopes sharing her story raises awareness about rabies risks from wild animal encounters A woman is praising an AI tool for helping her seek life-saving treatment. In May 2026, Laura Horton — a 53-year-old personal trainer from Liverpool, England — was celebrating her birthday with a two-week vacation to Lesbos, Greece. On the fourth day of the trip, she noticed a bat was drowning in the swimming pool at the apartment complex she was staying in. Not thinking much of it, she decided to rescue the animal. “I grabbed it from the water on instinct because it was drowning — I had no idea of the risks,” she told Southwest News Service. “It sat on my hand for about 10 minutes, drying off.” “We all thought it looked cute, so I took a photo of it,” she added. The villa where Laura was bitten. Laura Horton/SWNS Our new app is here! Free, fun and full of exclusives. Scan to download now! After the bat flew away, Horton said she uploaded the photo to Google Lens — an AI tool that allows users to search what they see using their device’s camera — to figure out what species of bat it was. However, the results that came up were far from expected. “It started asking questions like, ‘Are those your hands?’ and ‘Have you washed your hands for 15 minutes after handling it?’” she recalled. “It warned me of the risk of rabies and said I wouldn’t feel a bite, as their teeth are too small.” “I thought it was all a bit dramatic,” she admitted. “But when I took a photo of my hand, I saw two tiny bite marks. I had no idea you could catch rabies from a bat.” Laura Horton holding the bat. Laura Horton/SWNS Related Stories Rabies Is Making a Comeback. Should You Be Worried? Organ Donor Unknowingly Gave Rabies to Cornea Graft Recipient as Well as Kidney Recipient Who Died Horton decided to call her healthcare provider the following day, which confirmed that she should seek medical attention. She was informed that she would need to receive the rabies post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP) to prevent the virus from reaching her nervous system. She immediately went to a local hospital, but claimed that due to the language barrier, getting proper treatment was difficult. “At first, they tried to send me away with antibiotics. I’m not sure they understood what happened,” she said. “I was sent to a travel clinic, and a nurse was furious I hadn’t been given the first dose yet. I went back to the hospital and found the infectious diseases department – they were brilliant.” Bite marks on Laura Horton’s hand. Laura Horton/SWNS Sign up for From the Editor, our free weekly newsletter from PEOPLE’s Editor-in-Chief, Charlotte Triggs. While in Greece, Horton received two more shots before returning home on June 1 and receiving a fourth dose with her own doctor. “It was lucky Google Lens told me to go to hospital – who knows what might have happened,” she told the outlet. Laura Horton on vacation. Laura Horton/SWNS Never miss a story — sign up for PEOPLE’s free daily newsletter to stay up-to-date on the best of what PEOPLE has to offer​​, from celebrity news to compelling human interest stories. Horton said she’s been researching more about cases of rabies and is hoping that sharing her story will help others stay safe. “I read the tragic case about the boy in Canada who died after a bat landed on his face,” she said. “A lot of people aren’t aware of the dangers of these wild animals and the risks. Hopefully I can help raise awareness.” Laura Horton on vacation. Laura Horton/SWNS According to the Mayo Clinic, there’s no immediate sign that you’ve been infected with rabies, the deadly virus that’s nearly always spread to humans from contact with an infected animal. This is why doctors advise people to seek medical treatment immediately after any contact with a wild animal, and to undergo a series of rabies vaccines. Once symptoms begin to show, the condition is usually fatal. The incubation period depends on how far away from the brain the bite or scratch is, and how severe it is — and/or how much of the virus is transferred, the CDC warns. FCT 狂犬病爆发造成两人死亡 - THISDAYLIVE

Two Dead in FCT Rabies Outbreak - THISDAYLIVE
完整新闻中文摘要尼日利亚联邦首都区(FCT),特别是瓜瓦拉达(Gwagwalada)和夸利地区议会(Kwali Area Councils),已报告有两人因狂犬病咬伤而死亡。FCT 秘书处兽医服务主任 Karnak Dandam 博士在高原沃姆国家兽医研究所 (NVRI) 进行了样本检测,确认了 8 例病例,其中两人死亡。这次疫情的爆发与 2025 年 12 月和 2026 年 1 月由于疫苗短缺而疫苗接种活动不足有关。FCT政府已采取措施遏制疫情蔓延,包括采购更多疫苗剂量,并计划在六个地区委员会的所有村庄和城镇进行大规模疫苗接种活动。敦促居民报告被狗咬伤的情况,为宠物接种疫苗,并在接种疫苗后将其限制在规定范围内。
查看新闻五要素
时间
新闻报告发表于2026-07-16。报告的狂犬病病例发生在2026年3月和6月,第一批样本于3月采集,结果于3月确认;第二批样本于6月初采集,结果于6月15日确认。
地点与对象
疫情发生在尼日利亚联邦首都区(FCT),特别是瓜瓦拉达(Gwagwalada)和夸利(Kwali)地区议会。受影响的人群包括 FCT 的居民及其狗和猫。
事件
FCT 报告了由狂犬病狗咬伤引起的确诊狂犬病疫情,造成两人死亡。FCT 秘书处兽医服务主任 Karnak Dandam 博士证实了这一事件。
规模、影响与风险
FCT 已确诊 8 例狂犬病病例,造成 2 人死亡。FCT 政府表示,该病毒是致命的,一旦出现症状,生存机会很小。这次疫情的爆发与 2025 年 12 月和 2026 年 1 月由于疫苗短缺而疫苗接种活动不足有关。
应对、状态与不确定性
FCT政府已采取措施遏制疫情蔓延,包括采购更多疫苗剂量,并计划在六个地区委员会的所有村庄和城镇进行大规模疫苗接种活动。敦促居民报告被狗咬伤的情况,为宠物接种疫苗,并在接种疫苗后将其限制在规定范围内。来源是完整的原始报告,因此不确定性很低。

Two Dead in FCT Rabies Outbreak - THISDAYLIVE

Fetched Original Body
Menu Menu Home Business Politics Nigeria International 2Open submenu Health & Wellbeing Education Life & Style Sport Editorial Backpage Close submenuInternational Africa Rest of the World Sunday, 19th July, 2026 Home Business Politics Nigeria International Africa Rest of the World Health & Wellbeing Education Life & Style Sport Editorial Backpage Search for: Breaking News Breaking: Badejo-Okusanya Emerges NBA’s New National President 29 minutes ago Badejo-Okusanya Set to Emerge as NBA President 2 hours ago Breaking: Court Dashes Hope of Turaki-led PDP To Be Recognised By INEC, Dismisses Suit 2 days ago Breaking: FG Files 10-Count Charge Against Three Suspects over Oyo School Pupils, Teachers’ Abduction 2 days ago Latest Headlines betPawa Pays Out $1.6m from Its Locker Room Bonus Programme Breaking: Badejo-Okusanya Emerges NBA’s New National President Tinubu’s Upcoming Visit to Akwa Ibom Badejo-Okusanya Set to Emerge as NBA President Two Dead in FCT Rabies Outbreak Nigeria | 3 days ago Shares Share Post Email Share Share Olawale Ajimotokan in Abuja Religion& Belief Two persons have died of Rabies outbreak caused by a rabid dog bite in Abuja. The Director of Veterinary Services in the secretariat, Dr Karnak Dandam, confirmed the incident in Abuja, yesterday. The two rabies fatalities were reported in Gwagwalada and in Kwali Area Councils of the FCT out of eight cases that were recorded in two area councils. Dandam said measures were already taken to curb the spread and asked residents to report dog bites. Following the outbreak, the Agriculture and Rural Development Secretariat of the Federal Capital Territory Administration (FCTA) has urged residents of the territory to vaccinate their dogs and cats. Dandam said rabies is a deadly viral disease that attacks the central nervous system and transmitted through the bite of an infected dog or cat. He listed the symptoms of rabies in humans to include fever, headache, fatigue, and pain or tingling at the site of the bite, followed by anxiety, confusion, hallucinations and barking like a dog. Also he said infested dogs will exhibit symptoms, including excessive salivation, difficulty swallowing, hallucination, hanging jaws, aggression to the owner and a severe fear of water referred to as hydrophobia . He warned FCT residents that the virus is fatal, adding that once symptoms manifest in humans, the chances of survival is rare, adding the immediate remedy after a bite “is to initiate first aid by washing the bitten areas with soap and plenty of water and to immediately present the victim to medical personnel for treatment. He said that the reported cases were recorded in March and June, of which samples were taken to the National Veterinary Research Institute (NVRI), Vom, in Plateau for confirmation. “There are eight confirmed cases of samples taken for testing at the National Reference Laboratory, NVRI, and they came out positive. “The first samples we took to Vom this year was in March and the result came out the same month. The second samples were in early June and the result came out on June 15,” he said. He explained that the secretariat had carried out vaccination campaigns for dogs and cats across the FCT in December 2025 and January 2026, adding that the vaccines were inadequate due to shortage of the doses. Dogs He equally said that the secretariat had intensified its awareness campaigns, advising residents to confine their dogs and cats after vaccination to stop them from straying, as part of measures to prevent the spread of the virus. “The secretariat is also sensitising residents across the six area councils to take their dogs and cats to designated public and private veterinary clinics for rabies vaccination and certification. “Everybody has to restrict his or her dog and cats, so that they will not be roaming the streets and become exposed to infected rabid dogs,” he said. Dandam said that the FCT Administration had taken steps to procure the needed doses of the vaccines and would soon initiate a mass vaccination exercise in all villages and towns in the six area councils of the FCT to ensure that all dogs and cats are vaccinated in every community in the territory. “Anywhere there is a dog and a cat, we will visit, vaccinate and give the owners a certificate of vaccination and license. That’s our plan.” He urged residents to pay close attention to sudden changes in the behaviours of their dogs and cats which could be signs of rabies. “The major clinical sign that the owner will notice is that the dog that has been friendly suddenly becomes apprehensive and aggressive and starts attacking without provocation. “The first sign is that the dog will seclude itself, lay in a quiet place and will not be interested in any activity. “When the rabies gradually develops, the dog will suddenly become apprehensive and start attacking any object that it comes across, even the owner will not be spared. “As the disease advances, the dog will start roaming around with its jaw widely open, which will progress to paralysis and eventual death within 10 days of the onset of clinical signs,” he said. B routine priority

FCTA Confirms Eight Rabies Cases, Two Deaths, Issues Alert - Real Broadcasting Network

Fetched Original Body
The Federal Capital Territory Administration (FCTA) has confirmed eight rabies cases and two deaths in the territory, urging residents to vaccinate their dogs and cats to curb the virus’s spread The Agriculture and Rural Development Secretary of the FCTA confirmed the rabies outbreak on Wednesday in Abuja. The Director of Veterinary Services in the secretariat, Dr Karnak Dandam, told the News Agency of Nigeria (NAN) that two persons, one in Gwagwalada and the other in Kwali Area Councils of the FCT, died from rabid dog bites. Specifically, Dandam said that eight cases were recorded, five in Gwagwalada and three in Kwali Area Councils, out of which two persons lost their lives. He said measures were already taken to curb the spread and asked residents to report dog bites. Dandam described rabies as a “deadly viral disease that attacks the central nervous system” and is transmitted through the bite of an infected dog or cat. He said that the symptoms in humans include fever, headache, fatigue, and pain or tingling at the site of the bite, followed by anxiety, confusion, hallucinations and barking like a dog. In dogs, he said the symptoms include excessive salivation, difficulty swallowing, hallucinations, hanging jaws, aggression toward the owner and a severe fear of water referred to as hydrophobia. He warned that the virus is fatal, adding that once symptoms appear in humans, survival is rare. Dandam said the immediate remedy after a bite “is to wash the bite areas with soap and plenty of water as first aid and immediately present the victim to medical personnel for treatment”. The director said that the cases were recorded in March and June, adding that samples were taken to the National Veterinary Research Institute (NVRI), Vom, in Plateau for confirmation. “There are eight confirmed cases of samples taken for testing at the National Reference Laboratory, NVRI, and they came out positive. “The first samples we took to Vom this year were in March, and the result came out the same month. “The second samples were in early June, and the result came out on June 15,” he said He explained that the secretariat had carried out vaccination campaigns for dogs and cats across the FCT in Dec. 2025 and Jan. 2026, adding, however, that the vaccines could not go round due to shortage of the doses. The director equally said that the secretariat had intensified its awareness campaigns, advising residents to confine their dogs and cats after vaccination to stop them from straying, as part of measures to prevent the spread of the virus. “The secretariat is also sensitising residents across the six area councils to take their dogs and cats to designated public and private veterinary clinics for rabies vaccination and certification. “Everybody has to restrict his or her dog and cats, so that they will not be roaming the streets and become exposed to infected rabid dogs,” he said. Dandam said that the FCT Administration has taken steps to procure the needed doses of the vaccines and would soon begin a mass vaccination exercise in all villages and towns in the six area councils of the FCT. This, according to him, is to ensure that all dogs and cats are vaccinated in every community in the territory. “Anywhere there is a dog and a cat, we will visit, vaccinate and give the owners a certificate of vaccination and license. That’s our plan,’’ he said. The director also urged residents to pay close attention to sudden changes in the behaviours of their dogs and cats, which could be signs of rabies. “The major clinical sign that the owner will notice is that the friendly dog suddenly becomes apprehensive and aggressive and starts attacking without provocation. “The first sign is that the dog will seclude itself, lie in a quiet place and will not be interested in any activity. “When the rabies gradually develops, the dog will suddenly become apprehensive and start attacking any object that it comes across; even the owner will not be spared. “As the disease advances, the dog will start roaming around with its jaw widely open, which will progress to paralysis and eventual death within 10 days of the onset of clinical signs,” he said. Montana Officials Warn Pet Owners To Stay Alert As Rabies Cases Rise This Summer - KTVH

Fetched Original Body
News Actions Facebook Tweet Email Montana Officials Warn Pet Owners To Stay Alert As Rabies Cases Rise This Summer Prev Next By: Logan Cyr Posted 10:58 PM, Jul 14, 2026 HELENA — Montana livestock officials are warning residents to stay alert for possible rabies exposure this summer as reports involving bats and other wildlife continue across the state. The Montana Department of Livestock says bats are the most common source of rabies cases in Montana, while skunks are typically linked to cases in southeastern parts of the state. 1/1 Skip Ad Continue watching after the ad Visit Advertiser website GO TO PAGE “So in Montana the 2 types of wildlife that we see with rabies are skunks in the southeastern part of the state and then bats statewide,” said Dr. Emily Kaleczyc, assistant state veterinarian with the Department of Livestock. State officials say there have been three confirmed rabid bats in Montana so far this year, including cases in Yellowstone, Gallatin and Flathead counties. The department also says it receives several calls a day involving pets or people exposed to wildlife. “A lot of human exposures, especially in Montana, are related to people finding bats in homes or cabins,” Kaleczyc said. Officials say anyone who may have been bitten or exposed to a bat should contact a doctor or public health official right away. They also warn that rabies does not always cause aggressive behavior in animals. Pets or livestock infected with rabies may simply act differently than normal. “Any changes in behavior, especially anything neurologic related, can be a sign of rabies in domestic species,” Kaleczyc said. State veterinarians say keeping pets and livestock vaccinated is the best way to prevent the disease. “So we have very effective vaccines against rabies for all of our domestic species, dogs, cats, all of our livestock species,” Kaleczyc said. Officials encourage anyone with questions or concerns about possible rabies exposure to contact the Montana Department of Livestock at 406-444-2976 or visit the department’s website. (Photo: AdobeStock)Comment on this story2CommentShare storySharetopics: RabiesTehama CountyBatsPet VaccinationsPublic HealthAnimal BitePost-Exposure TreatmentCalifornia Department of Fish and WildlifeRight arrowTEHAMA — Two bats found in residential areas of Tehama County recently tested positive for rabies, prompting county health officials to remind residents to avoid contact with wild animals and ensure pets are vaccinated.The Tehama County Health Services Agency said rabies is a fatal viral disease that affects the central nervous system and can spread to humans and other mammals through the bite of an infected animal. Health officials said the disease is preventable if people take proper precautions and seek treatment promptly after a potential exposure.Officials urged residents not to handle, feed or attempt to capture unfamiliar, stray or wild animals, including bats and feral cats. Pet owners are encouraged to keep animals on a leash during outdoor activities and prevent them from interacting with wildlife.Children also should be taught not to approach unfamiliar animals, even if they appear friendly, according to the agency.Anyone bitten or scratched by an animal should immediately wash the wound thoroughly with soap and water and seek medical attention. Residents who believe they have been exposed to a potentially rabid animal should contact the Tehama County Health Services Agency Public Health Division at (530) 527-6824.Powered By 10 Sec 30.4K 2Two Tehama County bats test positive for rabies; officials urge pet vaccines and caution NextStayHealth officials also reminded pet owners to keep rabies vaccinations current through a licensed veterinarian.According to the agency, bats are the primary source of human rabies infections in the United States. While domestic animals account for fewer than 10% of reported rabies cases nationwide, rabies remains widespread among bats in Tehama County.Officials cautioned that rabid animals do not always display the commonly associated symptoms of excessive drooling or foaming at the mouth. Instead, infected animals may appear unusually aggressive or tame, stagger, become active during the day if they are normally nocturnal, or show other abnormal behavior.If left untreated, rabies is almost always fatal once symptoms develop. Early symptoms include fever, headache and fatigue before progressing to neurological symptoms such as confusion, hallucinations, difficulty swallowing and paralysis.Health officials said modern post-exposure treatment consists of four vaccine injections administered over two weeks, along with an injection of rabies immune globulin on the first day of treatment. The treatment is highly effective when started promptly after exposure.Residents who observe sick, injured or distressed wildlife are encouraged to report the animal to the California Department of Fish and Wildlife. Sick or injured domestic animals should be reported to the Tehama County Sheriff's Office.--Comment with BubblesJOIN THE CONVERSATION (2)Report a correction or typo. by Taboolaby TaboolaSPONSORED CONTENTWorrying About Blood Pressure? B routine priority

Rabies cases in RI are already outpacing recent years. What to know. - The Providence Journal

Fetched Original Body
LOCAL Rabies cases in RI are already outpacing recent years. What to know. Thirty-two humans have been exposed to rabies and received treatment in Rhode Island this year Jack Perry Providence Journal July 16, 2026, 3:13 p.m. ET Play Unmute -- / -- Full screen Keep Watching Rhode Island has seen a rise in rabies cases in 2026, surpassing the previous five-year average. State officials warn residents to avoid contact with wild animals to prevent exposure to the fatal disease. Raccoons and bats are the primary carriers of rabies identified in the state this year. An average of 24 animals in Rhode Island tested positive for rabies in each of the last five years, but 25 animals have already tested positive in 2026, with the year little more than half over. Thirty-two humans have been exposed to rabies and received treatment in Rhode Island this year, according to a joint press release from the Rhode Island Department of Health and the Rhode Island Department of Environmental Management. State officials took the opportunity on Thursday, July 16, to again remind Rhode Islanders about the importance of protecting themselves against the disease, which is fatal if not treated in time. Director of Health Dr. Jerry Larkin, said, “Being active in nature regularly is one of the best things you can do for your health. However, you should never touch a wild animal. "Any contact with a wild mammal puts you at risk for rabies.” Where have rabid animals been found? Rhode Island State Veterinarian Dr. Scott Marshall said, "Any direct contact with a wild mammal should be considered a potential rabies exposure." He said people should not approach or feed wild animals and should "report animals behaving unusually to authorities.” The majority of the rabid animals caught this year have been in the northern part of the state, according to state officials. Raccoons have accounted for the most, 14, followed by bats, 7. A rabid cat, coyote, skunk and a woodchuck have also tested positive this year, officials said. Rabies is most commonly transmitted through the bite of an infected animal, but it can also spread when saliva from a rabid animal enters the body through a scratch or an open wound, according to state officials. State law requires all dogs, cats and ferrets to be vaccinated against rabies. State officials say vaccinating pets prevents them from getting rabies and prevents people from becoming exposed to rabies through their pets. The health department recommends the following additional steps to prevent rabies: Avoid all contact with and do not feed stray or free-roaming domestic animals. Avoid all contact with and do not feed wild animals. Do not feed your animals outdoors, as this will attract other animals. This is especially dangerous when feeding large numbers of free-roaming cats. Protect your pets by always maintaining control; walk dogs on a leash or let them play in a fenced yard; and do not let pets wander unsupervised. Securely cover all garbage cans so wild animals cannot scavenge for food. What to do if a person or pet comes into contact with a wild animal Anyone who has had direct contact with a wild mammal should immediately call RIDOH's Center for Acute Infectious Disease Epidemiology at 401-222-2577 (Monday to Friday, 8:30 a.m. to 4:30 p.m. or 401-276-8046 after hours). A RIDOH nurse will assess the exposure and determine whether post-exposure treatment is recommended. If needed, RIDOH will coordinate a post-exposure vaccine series. If any domestic animal, such as pets or livestock, had contact with a wild mammal, or if you are in possession of an animal that you suspect has rabies, call RIDOH, your local animal control officer, and your veterinarian. What a doctor says 'Boo Boo' Andrade eyes comeback after family tragedy Bats sometimes get inside homes, and testing of the bat or preventive vaccination is recommended when a bat has been found in a home where people or pets have been sleeping, or if the bat was around people who do not know if they had contact with the bat. For more information on how to prevent rabies, visit RIDOH’s website. For more information on rabies and ways to protect yourself and your pets, please see this RIDOH fact sheet. Raccoons Test Positive for Rabies in July; 8 Total this Season July 13, 2026 Sophia Peters, UPDATE: The Wicomico County Health Department has announced two more positive rabies cases found in raccoons this July, making the total number of positive cases eight this year. On Jul. 2, a raccoon tested positive for rabies in the area of Nithsdale on Caledonia Drive in Salisbury. Less than two weeks later, on Jul. 13, another raccoon tested positive for rabies; this time in the area of Archie Dennis Road in Parsonsburg after contact with a dog. The Wicomico County Health Department continues to advise all residents to be aware of their surroundings and avoid contact with all wild and/or stray animals. UPDATE: The Wicomico County Health Department (WCHD) has announced that another raccoon has tested positive for rabies, making it the fifth rabies case in Wicomico this year. On Friday, Jun. 12, a raccoon tested positive for rabies in the area of Kensington Woods and Upper Ferry Road in Salisbury Maryland, after contact with a dog. The Wicomico County Health Department is advising all residents to be aware of their surroundings and avoid contact with all wild and/or stray animals. This update was published on Ju. 12 at 5:00 p.m. UPDATE: The Wicomico County Health Department (WCHD) has announced that another raccoon has tested positive for rabies, making it the fourth case in the county in just two months. On Tuesday, Apr. 28, a raccoon tested positive for rabies in the area of Hazel Street and Old Ocean City Road in Salisbury after having contact with a dog. The Wicomico County Health Department is advising all residents to be aware of their surroundings and avoid contact with all wild and/or stray animals. This update was published on Apr. 28 at 3:28 p.m. WILLARDS, Md. – The Wicomico County Health Department (WCHD) has announced that a third raccoon in the county has tested positive for rabies. Officials say the raccoon was found near in the area of Rayne Road in Willards after it had contact with a dog. Two other rabid raccoons were located this month in Parsonsburg and Delmar. The WCHD is advising all residents to be aware of their surroundings and avoid contact with wild/stray animals. They also offer the following tips to protect your family and pets: Keep pets confined and avoid feeding them outside Teach children to leave unfamiliar animals alone Vaccinate pets Secure outside garbage bins Rabies is a preventable viral disease that exists in and is transmitted by the saliva of mammals. Maryland law requires all dogs, cats, and ferrets aged four months or older to have a current rabies vaccination. If left untreated, rabies is fatal. To report human or animal contact, such as bites or scratches, call WCHD at (410)-546-4446. 事件
— 莱恩县公共卫生局 (LCPH) 周一宣布,俄勒冈州立大学兽医诊断实验室证实在莱恩县发现了一只感染狂犬病的蝙蝠。
规模、影响与风险
It is almost 100 percent fatal once symptoms begin.
应对、状态与不确定性
今年到目前为止,俄勒冈州已有 13 只蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性。今年在莱恩县发现了第三只狂犬病呈阳性的蝙蝠;官员敦促宠物疫苗莱恩县居民和兽医应意识到其他动物可能接触狂犬病病毒,因此应警惕该疾病的潜在迹象。

officials urge pet vaccinesby News StaffMon, July 13, 2026 at 3:34 PMUpdated Tue, July 14, 2026 at 10:05 AM(File: Getty Images)Comment on this story0CommentShare storySharetopics: RabiesLane CountyPublic HealthBatPet VaccinationOregonVeterinary Diagnostic LaboratoryRabies PreventionRight arrowEUGENE, Ore. — Lane County Public Health (LCPH) on Monday announced laboratory confirmation from the Veterinary Diagnostic Laboratory at Oregon State University of a rabies-infected bat found in Lane County. This is the third bat found in Lane County which has tested positive for rabies in 2026."All pet owners should make certain their dogs and cats are vaccinated against rabies. When our pets are protected from rabies, it provides a buffer zone of immune animals between humans and rabid wild animals, such as bats," says Dr. Emilio DeBess, State Public Health Veterinarian.RELATED | Rabid bat found in Marion County prompts county health warningWhile bats help control insect populations, including mosquitoes, they are the primary reservoir of rabies in our wild animal communities, the county explained. Thus far this year in Oregon 13 bats have tested positive for rabies.Third rabies-positive bat found in Lane County this year; officials urge pet vaccinesLane County residents and veterinarians should be aware that other animals could be exposed to the rabies virus and should, therefore, be alert to potential signs of the disease. Rabies is a viral disease that affects the nervous system of humans and mammals. It is almost 100 percent fatal once symptoms begin. The virus is carried in the saliva of an infected animal; transmission can occur when that animal bites, or in rare instances, scratches another animal or person. If bitten by a bat the wound should be immediately and thoroughly cleaned with soap and water. In addition, medical attention should be sought. Finally, if easily accomplished, the bat should be captured and the event reported to the Lane County Public Health.Powered By 1034 0Sunshine & 90s in the ValleysShareNextStayThere are several strains of rabies found in the U.S. (e.g., bat, skunk, fox, raccoon) but only bat rabies is found in Oregon. Other animals that have tested positive for rabies in Oregon have contracted it from bats. People should stay away from bats and not handle them.LCPH recommends the following:Vaccinate your pets (dogs and cats) against rabiesDo not handle bats with bare handsWatch wildlife from a distance. Do not approach or attempt to handle wild animals.Do not feed wild animalsKeep garbage in secure containers and away from wildlifeFeed pets indoorsSeal openings in attics, basements, porches, sheds, barns and screen chimneys that might provide access to bats and other wildlifeIf your pet has contact with a bat, contact your veterinarianComment with BubblesBE THE FIRST TO COMMENTFor a statewide map showing the distribution of rabies in bats for 2026, see below:Click here to view the PDF file by Taboolaby TaboolaSPONSORED CONTENTTimeshare Resorts Forced To Refund Owners - Is Yours On the List?SilverPenny | SponsoredSponsoredUndoJust Add 1 Drop Of This Household Item To Any Dark Spot and Wait 3 MinutesGundry MD | SponsoredSponsoredUndoAsk a Pro: "I'm 65 With $1.1M Saved. B routine priority

Health minister approves national rabies control framework - The Nation (Pakistan )

Fetched Original Body
nawaiwaqt group News Paper & TV Channel Roznama Nawaiwaqt Waqt News TV Magazines Sunday Magazine Family Magazine Nidai Millat Mahnama Phool Sunday, July 19, 2026 e-Paper Today's Paper EDITOR'S PICKS Newspaper Picks OPINION Editorials Columns Letters NEWS Latest Headlines Top Stories National Sports Lifestyle & Entertainment Business International Editor's Picks Snippets Lahore Karachi Islamabad MULTIMEDIA News In Pictures Cartoons Videos BLOGS Blogs Write for Us AI Use Policy CONNECT WITH US Get Alerts Health minister approves national rabies control framework Web Desk 2:39 PM | July 13, 2026 National Share on Facebook Share on Twitter Share on Facebook Share on Twitter ISLAMABAD: Federal Minister for National Health Services, Regulations and Coordination Mustafa Kamal on Monday voiced serious concern over the increasing number of rabies cases and dog bite incidents across Pakistan, calling for urgent nationwide measures to tackle the preventable yet deadly disease. The minister made the remarks during a meeting with a delegation from the Indus Hospital & Health Network (IHHN), led by its Chief Executive Officer Dr Abdul Bari Khan, to discuss a comprehensive national strategy for rabies prevention and control. During the meeting, the delegation briefed the minister on the country's rabies situation, highlighting the absence of an effective national rabies surveillance system. FFD issues high flood alert for Chenab, Jhelum rivers According to estimates shared during the briefing, around 5,000 people die from rabies each year in Pakistan, while hundreds of thousands of dog bite cases are reported annually. Children and residents of rural and underserved urban areas are among the most vulnerable. Expressing deep concern over the growing burden of the disease, Mustafa Kamal gave in-principle approval for the development of a National Rabies Prevention and Control Framework aimed at strengthening prevention, surveillance and response efforts across the country. He also announced the formation of a National Technical Working Advisory Group, which will lead the preparation of the framework and coordinate nationwide efforts to combat rabies. Man arrested after firing incident near Attaullah Tarar's vehicle in Lahore The health minister stressed the need for a nationwide public awareness campaign to educate citizens about rabies prevention, the importance of seeking immediate medical treatment after animal bites, and timely vaccination. During the meeting, Dr Abdul Bari Khan presented the minister with the "Sindh Official Guidelines for Prevention of Human Rabies (2024)," developed with the technical assistance of The Indus Hospital and Health Network. The meeting was held days after another rabies case was reported in Karachi. On July 10, a 28-year-old woman, identified as Malookan Bibi from Khwaja Ajmer Nagri in North Karachi, was admitted to the emergency department of Jinnah Postgraduate Medical Centre (JPMC) after developing symptoms of the fatal viral disease. Read Full Bio Dan Raby July 13, 2026 / 1:10 PM EDT / CBS Atlanta Add CBS News on Google A raccoon that had been attacked by multiple dogs last week has tested positive for rabies, Gwinnett County officials say.Authorities say the attack happened on July 8 near Tree View Drive in Snellville.The three dogs involved in the attack are now undergoing evaluation and rabies post-exposure management by veterinarians. Georgia is a rabies-endemic state, which means the virus can be found in wildlife year-round. Officials say that means it's important for residents to make sure their pets are up-to-date on their vaccinations and avoid animals that may be behaving in unusual ways. In June, a raccoon in the Bethlehem area of Gwinnett County tested positive for rabies.The National Association of State Health Veterinarians says all unvaccinated dogs and cats exposed to a rabid animal need to be quarantined for four months and vaccinated one month before being released.If you have been bitten or scratched by any stray animals or an animal suspected to have rabies, seek medial care immediately and let your provider know about the possible exposure. After that, contact GNR Public Health at (770) 339-4260 and ask for the on-call epidemiologist.To report an animal behaving in an unusual way, call the Gwinnett Animal Welfare and Enforcement Bite Office at (770) 339-3200, ext. 5576. B 常规重要

南卡罗来纳州各县两只动物狂犬病检测呈阳性 - WYFF

Two animals test positive for rabies in South Carolina counties - WYFF
完整新闻中文摘要据 2026 年 7 月 17 日发布的当地新闻报道,南卡罗来纳州格林伍德县和乔治敦县的两只动物(一只浣熊和一只狐狸)狂犬病检测呈阳性。确诊病例引发了人们对受影响地区人类或家畜潜在暴露风险的担忧。公共卫生当局尚未在现有报告中发布具体指导或应对措施。该事件仍在调查中,没有提供有关测试、隔离或预防措施的进一步细节。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-17。事件日期:所提供证据中未报告。
地点与对象
美国南卡罗来纳州;格林伍德县和乔治敦县。
事件
两只动物狂犬病检测呈阳性:一只浣熊和一只狐狸。当地媒体WYFF报道。状态:确诊阳性病例。
规模、影响与风险
两例确诊的动物狂犬病病例(一只浣熊,一只狐狸)。没有人类病例或接触的报道。风险:受影响县的人类或家畜可能存在接触。
应对、状态与不确定性
公共卫生当局尚未在提供的证据中发布具体指导。没有提供有关检测、隔离、接触者追踪或预防措施的详细信息。目前状态:确诊病例正在调查中。

GREENWOOD, S.C. — The South Carolina Department of Public Health has confirmed two animals from different counties have tested positive for rabies. Officials say in Greenwood County, a raccoon was found near Blue Jay and Stevenson Roads that tested positive for rabies. One dog was exposed and is expected to be quarantined in compliance with the South Carolina Rabies Control Act. Advertisement Spartanburg woman sentenced to 26 years in boyfriend’s killing In Georgetown County, a fox was found near Plantersville Road and North Fraser Street that tested positive for rabies. One person and one dog were exposed and have been referred for care and quarantine. Clemson restaurant owners, employee arrested in human trafficking investigation “Keeping your pets and livestock current on their rabies vaccination is a responsibility that comes with owning an animal. It is one of the easiest and most effective ways you can protect yourself, your family, your pets, and your livestock from this fatal disease. That is an investment worth making to provide yourself some peace of mind,” said Terri McCollister, DPH’s rabies program manager. Town of Pendleton under boil water advisory until further notice If you believe you, someone you know, or your pets have come in contact with this raccoon or this fox, or another animal that potentially has rabies, please call DPH's rabies 24-hour rabies reporting line at 888-847-0902. Careers Asheville health officials advise Candler animal rescue visitors to seek rabies evaluation Local health officials have advised anyone who has visited Alleyoop Wildlife Rescue and Rehabilitation, an Asheville-area animal rescue, within the past six months to seek a rabies risk assessment. By 828newsNOW July 16, 2026 3:23 pm Facebook X More LinkedIn Email SMS Associated PressAP Photo/Hans Pennink, FileFILE - Oral rabies vaccine bait that will be dropped by a state police helicopter from the New York State Police Aviation Unit in Albany and team members of the State Health Department is displayed during a rabies vaccine bait drop over the Northeastern part of the state during the New York State Department of Health Racoon Vaccination Project, Monday, Aug. 20, 2007. The U.S. government has begun scattering millions of packets of oral rabies vaccine from helicopters and planes over 13 states from Maine to Alabama. The major aim is to keep raccoons from spreading their strain of the deadly virus to states where it hasn't been found or isn't widespread, said field trial coordinator Jordona Kirby. CANDLER, N.C. (828newsNOW) — Local health officials have advised anyone who has visited Alleyoop Wildlife Rescue and Rehabilitation, an Asheville-area animal rescue, within the past six months to seek a rabies risk assessment. Buncombe County officials alerted visitors to Michelle Cole or Alleyoop Wildlife Rescue and Rehabilitation, located in the Sand Hill Road area of Candler, that they should contact their local health department for a rabies risk assessment if they have visited the practice or had contact with wildlife there since February 2026. “In the State of North Carolina, licensed wildlife rehabilitators must possess a Captivity License for Rehabilitation, an additional rabies vector species certification and have a complete rabies pre-exposure series to be allowed to handle and rehabilitate animal species that are at high risk of carrying rabies virus,” Buncombe County said in a press release. Rabies-carrying species include: Bats Bobcats Foxes Raccoons Skunks In Buncombe County, and other counties where the United States Department of Agriculture Oral Rabies Vaccination program is enacted, rehabilitating and releasing those vector species is not authorized, with the exception of bats. “Unlicensed individuals are not allowed to possess or provide care to wildlife due to safety and public health concerns,” the county said. Officials advise the following precautions for preventing rabies exposure: Do not approach, feed or handle wildlife. Leave orphaned wildlife alone. If a wild animal or stray pet near your home appears sick or is behaving unusually, or if you find a bat inside your home, contact the North Carolina Wildlife Resources Commission Wildlife Helpline at (866) 318-2401 and HWI@ncwildlife.gov, or your county animal control. Keep dogs, cats, ferrets and livestock up to date on rabies vaccinations. Do not feed pets outdoors. Supervise pets when they are outside. A fenced yard provides the best protection. If you think you have been exposed to animals at this facility, contact the Buncombe County Health & Human Services – Communicable Disease staff at (828) 250-5109. Residents of other counties should contact the local health department in their county of residence. For more information on rabies prevention and wildlife, residents are encouraged to contact the NC Wildlife Resources Commission at (866) 318-2401. Facebook X More LinkedIn Email SMS That cool break was short-lived: Asheville’s heat returns this week Asheville will see highs near 90 degrees, with afternoon thunderstorms possible Friday through Sunday By Dee Pridgen July 16, 2026 12:31 pmSponsored by Precision International Facebook X More LinkedIn Email SMS ContributedSpecial to 828newsNOWSunset Thursday near Alexander. ASHEVILLE, N.C. (828newsNOW) — After a brief break from the summer heat, warmer temperatures are returning to Western North Carolina. Highs are expected to climb back into the upper 80s and low 90s this week, with heat index values approaching triple digits in parts of the region. The National Weather Service Greenville-Spartanburg office said hot conditions will return Thursday and continue through the weekend. While mountain valleys, including Asheville, are expected to stay a little cooler than areas farther east, temperatures will still run above normal. Thursday’s forecast for Asheville calls for mostly sunny skies with a high near 90 degrees. Temperatures will increase slightly Friday, with a high near 91 and a chance of afternoon showers and thunderstorms. The hottest conditions are expected across the Piedmont and foothills, where heat index values could reach 100 to 105 degrees from Thursday through Sunday. Forecasters said heat advisories could become possible, especially in areas farther east. The weather service is reminding people to take precautions during the heat, including staying hydrated, limiting strenuous outdoor activities during the hottest part of the day and checking on vulnerable neighbors and family members. A cold front moving in from the north could bring some relief early next week. Before then, rain chances will increase over the weekend. Asheville has an 80 percent chance of showers and thunderstorms Saturday, with additional chances Sunday afternoon. Temperatures are expected to ease slightly, with highs in the upper 80s to near 90 through the weekend. Forecasters said storms will be most likely during the afternoon and evening hours, especially across the mountains. Some storms could bring heavy rain, gusty winds or isolated severe weather. The return of hot weather comes as the region remembers another extreme weather event. The National Weather Service noted that 110 years ago this week, the Great Flood of 1916 devastated parts of Western North Carolina. Widespread flooding and debris flows were reported throughout the French Broad River basin, and the state’s record for 24-hour rainfall was set when Altapass in Mitchell County recorded 22.22 inches of rain.
