The raccoon was sent to the New York State Department of Health Wadsworth Center for testing on July 15 and positive results were reported on July 16. Two humans were exposed to the raccoon. B 常规重要

渥太华县今年首例蝙蝠狂犬病病例 - AOL.com

First Ottawa County rabies case of the year found in bat - AOL.com
完整新闻中文摘要2026 年 7 月 15 日,渥太华县公共卫生部确认了密歇根州 2026 年首例动物狂犬病病例,此前荷兰镇的一只蝙蝠检测结果呈阳性。州数据显示,今年全州出现 21 例蝙蝠狂犬病病例,但没有报告人类感染病例。官员警告称,从五月到九月,接触风险会增加,并建议居民不要接触野生动物,不要给宠物接种疫苗,如果被咬或抓伤,请立即就医。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于2026年7月16日,报道了周三(2026年7月15日)一只蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性的情况。
地点与对象
密歇根州渥太华县,特别是荷兰镇。受影响的人群包括当地居民及其宠物。
事件
2026 年 7 月 15 日,渥太华县公共卫生部确认了该县今年首例动物狂犬病病例,此前一只蝙蝠检测呈阳性。
规模、影响与风险
截至周一(2026 年 7 月 14 日),全州已确诊 21 例蝙蝠狂犬病病例,奥克兰县还有 1 例臭鼬病例。密歇根州今年尚未报告人间病例。五月至九月的暴露风险较高。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生官员敦促居民切勿接触野生动物,为宠物接种疫苗,如果接触野生动物,请立即寻求治疗。卫生部门没有具体说明发现蝙蝠的具体地点和时间。
B routine priority

First Ottawa County rabies case of the year found in bat - AOL.com

Fetched Original Body
HOLLAND TOWNSHIP, Mich. (WOOD) — The first Ottawa County animal case of rabies this year has been discovered after a bat tested positive for the disease Wednesday. The Ottawa County Department of Public Health did not specify where or when the bat was found, saying only it had been informed of the positive rabies test Wednesday. As of Monday, 21 cases of rabies have been found in bats across the state, with only one case of rabies being a skunk found in Oakland County, state data shows . No humans have contracted rabies in Michigan yet this year. The risk of exposure to rapid bats is higher from May through September when bats are most active. Health officials say to never touch or handle wild animals and urge people to vaccinate their pets against rabies. Exposure to rabies happens when people or pets are scratched or bitten by a rabid animal. Animals with rabies do not always appear sick and animals suspected of having rabies should be sent in for lab testing. If you believe you have found an animal that has rabies, contact the Ottawa County Department of Public Health or your local pest control. You can also bring an animal to the Harbor Humane Society for testing if you are able to catch it without making physical contact. If you suspect that you have been exposed to a rabid animal, even while asleep, you should seek treatment immediately, health officials say. Pets who have been exposed to a rabid animal should also receive veterinary care even if they are vaccinated against the disease. Copyright 2026 Nexstar Media, Inc. All rights reserved. This material may not be published, broadcast, rewritten, or redistributed. For the latest news, weather, sports, and streaming video, head to WOODTV.com
View news five-element analysis
Time
The article was published on July 16, 2026, reporting on a bat testing positive for rabies on Wednesday (July 15, 2026).
Location and population
Ottawa County, Michigan, specifically Holland Township. The affected population includes local residents and their pets.
Event
The Ottawa County Department of Public Health confirmed the first animal rabies case of the year in the county after a bat tested positive on July 15, 2026.
Scale, impact and risk
As of Monday (July 14, 2026), there have been 21 confirmed rabies cases in bats statewide, with one additional case in a skunk in Oakland County. No human cases have been reported in Michigan this year. The risk of exposure is higher from May through September.
Response, status and uncertainty
Health officials urge residents never to touch wild animals, vaccinate pets, and seek immediate treatment if exposed. The specific location and time of the bat's discovery were not specified by the health department.

Source: ABC30 Fresno  |  Published: 2026-07-19
B 常规重要

弗雷斯诺查菲动物园证实在场地内发现了 2 只狂犬病呈阳性的野生蝙蝠 - ABC30 弗雷斯诺

Fresno Chaffee Zoo confirms 2 rabies-positive wild bats found on grounds - ABC30 Fresno
完整新闻中文摘要弗雷斯诺查菲动物园证实本月在其场地内发现了两只狂犬病呈阳性的野生蝙蝠,这是该设施中发现的首例病例。动物园在对死去的蝙蝠进行测试后报告了这一发现,但没有披露人类或动物的暴露情况。虽然狂犬病传播的风险已得到承认,但动物园在提供的报告中没有提供有关调查步骤、公共卫生通知或预防措施的进一步细节。蝙蝠发现的确切时间以及任何后续行动尚未报道。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-19。事件日期:所提供证据中未报告(陈述为“本月”)
地点与对象
弗雷斯诺查菲动物园,弗雷斯诺县,加利福尼亚州,美国。
事件
弗雷斯诺查菲动物园本月报告了两起在动物园内发现的死亡野生蝙蝠的狂犬病确诊病例。该事件已得到动物园证实。
规模、影响与风险
鉴定出两只狂犬病呈阳性的野生蝙蝠。所提供的证据中没有报告人类或动物接触或病例。由于动物园内存在受感染的蝙蝠,狂犬病传播给人类或动物的风险被明确指出为令人担忧的问题。
应对、状态与不确定性
动物园证实了这些病例;所提供的证据中没有报告进一步的调查、测试或公共卫生应对细节。目前正在调查潜在的暴露风险。未解决的信息包括确切的事件日期、暴露细节以及采取的任何公共卫生行动。
B routine priority

For our other digital properties, to opt out, select “Do Not Sell or Share My Personal Information” or “Your Privacy Choices” on each. You may enable online, where available, a universal tool that automatically communicates your opt-out, such as Global Privacy Control (“GPC”), which we’ll process as if you had opted out through the toggle.Even if you opt out, you may continue to see advertising, including ads that may be based on personal information processed before you opted out. 第三巷县蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性 - NPR for Oregonians

Third Lane County bat tests positive for rabies - NPR for Oregonians
完整新闻中文摘要莱恩县公共卫生部门周一证实,该县发现的一只蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性,这是莱恩县今年第三起此类病例。狂犬病是一种攻击神经系统的致命病毒,通过动物唾液或抓痕传播。卫生官员敦促居民注意攻击性或迷失方向等迹象,与野生动物保持距离,安全存放垃圾,并密封房屋开口以防止野生动物进入。特别建议宠物主人确保他们的动物接种疫苗。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 13 日下午 3:56(太平洋夏令时间);周一宣布(活动日期未指定,从公告上下文推断)。
地点与对象
美国俄勒冈州莱恩县。受影响的人群包括该县的居民和潜在的宠物主人。
事件
莱恩县公共卫生局 (LCPH) 宣布发现一只感染狂犬病的蝙蝠。这是莱恩县今年发现的第三只病毒检测呈阳性的蝙蝠。该事件已确认。
规模、影响与风险
这是莱恩县 2026 年第三例确诊的蝙蝠狂犬病病例。狂犬病是一种由攻击神经系统的病毒引起的致命疾病。病毒通过动物的唾液传播,有时也通过抓痕传播。LCPH 敦促宠物主人为他们的动物接种疫苗,并敦促居民留意疾病迹象,例如攻击性、迷失方向或过度流口水。建议包括与野生动物保持距离、安全存放垃圾以及密封房屋开口以防止野生动物进入。
应对、状态与不确定性
LCPH 已宣布这一发现,并积极敦促宠物主人为动物接种疫苗,并敦促居民留意疾病迹象。来源是 NPR for Oregonians (KLCC) 的完整原始报告。

Milestones News Awards Public Records Signal Status Streaming Options People Employment Opportunities About KLCC KLCC Stations & Translators Contest Rules Business Underwriting Contact Us KLCC Public Radio Foundation Listener Resources Milestones News Awards Public Records Signal Status Streaming Options People Employment Opportunities News Briefs Third Lane County bat tests positive for rabies KLCC Published July 13, 2026 at 3:56 PM PDT Twitter LinkedIn Email Michael Durham / Oregon Department of Fish and WildlifeBats are the primary rabies vector species in the U.S. Note: the bat pictured here is not sick. Lane County Public Health announced Monday that they found a rabies-infected bat. This is the third bat found in Lane County this year to test positive for the virus.Rabies is a deadly disease caused by a virus that attacks the nervous system. The virus is spread through the saliva of animals, and sometimes scratches.Animals other than bats could be exposed to rabies, and LCPH is urging pet owners to make sure their animals are vaccinated against the virus.Residents should also look out for signs of the disease, such as aggression, disorientation or excessive drooling.Other recommendations include keeping distance from wild animals, storing garbage in secure containers and sealing openings in your home that wildlife may be able to access. Tags News Briefs Rabiesbat rabiesbatsLane County Twitter LinkedIn Email
View news five-element analysis
Time
Published July 13, 2026 at 3:56 PM PDT; announced Monday (event date unspecified, inferred from announcement context).
Location and population
Lane County, Oregon, USA. Affected population includes residents and potentially pet owners in the county.
Event
Lane County Public Health (LCPH) announced the discovery of a rabies-infected bat. This is the third bat found in Lane County this year to test positive for the virus. The event is confirmed.
Scale, impact and risk
This is the third confirmed bat case of rabies in Lane County in 2026. Rabies is a deadly disease caused by a virus that attacks the nervous system. The virus is spread through the saliva of animals, and sometimes scratches. LCPH is urging pet owners to vaccinate their animals and residents to look out for signs of the disease, such as aggression, disorientation, or excessive drooling. Recommendations include keeping distance from wild animals, storing garbage securely, and sealing home openings to prevent wildlife access.
Response, status and uncertainty
LCPH has announced the finding and is actively urging pet owners to vaccinate animals and residents to watch for signs of the disease. The source is a full original report from NPR for Oregonians (KLCC).

Source: nny360.com  |  Published: 2026-07-18

美国纽约州蒙哥马利县。受影响的人群:纽约州富尔顿维尔的一匹马。宿主:浣熊狂犬病株。
事件
纽约州蒙哥马利县的一匹马狂犬病检测呈阳性。蒙哥马利县卫生部门证实存在浣熊狂犬病株。该事件已确认。
规模、影响与风险
这匹马需要花费数千美元的救生疫苗。该地区存在浣熊狂犬病株,对当地社区和动物构成风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
蒙哥马利县卫生局分享了实验室结果和菌株信息。所提供的证据中没有充分详细说明马匹的康复状况以及对公众的风险程度。

事件
2026 年 7 月 11 日,圣马科斯的一只狐狸攻击一只流浪猫后,狂犬病检测呈阳性。该事件已报告给圣马科斯动物服务中心。

San Marcos fox tests positive for rabies

Fetched Original Body
San Marcos fox tests positive for rabies Editor's note: The video above is previous coverage from April 7, 2026. AUSTIN (KXAN) — A report to San Marcos Animal Services said a fox with rabies attacked a stray cat on July 11 in Willow Creek Estates, according to a release. The fox was captured, but Animal Services could not find the cat, which was described as having short, black and white hair with a wound to its hindquarters. Officials said they aren't sure if the cat is owned or has any history of rabies vaccination. Officials said the cat ran into the greenbelt between the Willow Creek Estates and the Siesta Verde neighborhood. Animal Services has not been able to find the cat since the day of the fox attack. It is unknown if the cat is owned or has a rabies vaccination. Rabies is a deadly virus that is spread through a bite and can infect mammals, including wildlife, pets, and humans. Wild animals that appear to be sick, or any physical contact with a wild animal, should be immediately reported to Animal Protection Officers by contacting Animal Services at 512-805-2655. A report from Animal Services encouraged pet owners in the community to vaccinate their pets against rabies. For resources and information about obtaining low-cost rabies vaccinations for pets, visit www.sanmarcostx.gov/pethelp. Any cat matching the description should be immediately reported to Animal Services at 512-805-2655 to help stop the spread of the virus. Copyright 2026 Nexstar Media, Inc. All rights reserved. This material may not be published, broadcast, rewritten, or redistributed. For the latest news, weather, sports, and streaming video, head to KXAN Austin

Scale, impact and risk

圣地亚哥动物园野生动物园发现感染狂犬病的蝙蝠 - 补丁

Rabies-Infected Bat Found At San Diego Zoo Safari Park - Patch
完整新闻中文摘要2026 年 7 月 18 日星期六,在加利福尼亚州埃斯孔迪多的圣地亚哥动物园野生动物园发现的一只野生蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性。这是继 2025 年出现 21 例狂犬病阳性病例之后,今年圣地亚哥县发现的第 19 只蝙蝠。县卫生官员在圣地亚哥县公共卫生实验室进行检测后证实了这一结果。目前还没有人与人接触的报道,但官员们敦促任何可能接触过蝙蝠的人立即联系公共卫生服务部门。如果不治疗,狂犬病几乎 100% 致命,因此早期医疗干预至关重要。
查看新闻五要素
时间
中午12点10分,蝙蝠被发现。星期六(活动日期)。文章发表于2026年7月16日(发表日期)。测试于周一得到确认。
地点与对象
圣地亚哥动物园位于加利福尼亚州埃斯孔迪多。受影响的人群包括公众和公园游客,特别是那些可能接触过蝙蝠的人。这只蝙蝠是一种野生动物,不属于公园的收藏品。
事件
在圣地亚哥动物园野生动物园发现的一只野生蝙蝠狂犬病检测呈阳性。这是县卫生官员和 Patch 媒体报道的已证实事件。
规模、影响与风险
这是今年(2026 年)圣地亚哥县第 19 只狂犬病检测呈阳性的蝙蝠。2025年,有21只蝙蝠检测呈阳性。没有人与人接触的报道。对于直接接触的人来说,这种风险被认为很高,因为如果不及时治疗,狂犬病几乎 100% 致命。
应对、状态与不确定性
县卫生官员正在进行调查,以确保任何接触过蝙蝠的人都能迅速得到护理。他们敦促任何接触过蝙蝠的人致电县公共卫生服务部门,电话:619-692-8499。尚未报告人间病例。来源是 Patch 的完整文章。
B routine priority

Rabies-Infected Bat Found At San Diego Zoo Safari Park - Patch

Fetched Original Body
Community Corner Rabies-Infected Bat Found At San Diego Zoo Safari Park The bat is the 19th to test positive for rabies this year in San Diego County. ESCONDIDO, CA — A wild bat found at the San Diego Zoo Safari Park tested positive for rabies, prompting county health officials to ask anyone who may have had contact with the animal to come forward. The deceased bat was found at 12:10 p.m. Saturday at the Oasis on the Rocks restaurant at the Escondido-based park. The animal was collected by a trained Safari Park employee. No human contact with the bat has been reported. The bat was not part of the park's animal collection. It was sent to the San Diego County Public Health Laboratory on Monday, where testing confirmed it had rabies. Find out what's happening in San Diegofor free with the latest updates from Patch. Anyone who touched the bat, or knows someone who may have, was urged to call County Public Health Services at 619-692-8499. Officials said only people who had direct contact with the animal are considered at risk. The bat is the 19th to test positive for rabies this year in the county. In 2025, 21 bats tested positive. Find out what's happening in San Diegofor free with the latest updates from Patch. "Rabies is an extremely serious viral disease, one of the deadliest, with nearly 100% fatality if not caught early enough," said Dr. Ankita Kadakia, the county's deputy public health officer. "We are investigating to ensure anyone who came into contact with the bat receives care quickly. Once symptoms start there is no treatment for rabies." Health officials reminded residents not to touch bats or other wildlife. Anyone who has direct contact with a bat should wash the area with soap and seek medical advice. Rabies symptoms can take weeks or months to appear, but treatment is highly effective when started before symptoms begin. Once symptoms develop, the disease is almost always fatal, officials said. Time
The bat was found at 12:10 p.m. on Saturday (event date). The article was published on July 16, 2026 (publication date). Testing was confirmed on Monday.
Location and population
San Diego Zoo Safari Park in Escondido, California. The affected population includes the general public and park visitors, specifically those who may have had contact with the bat. The bat was a wild animal, not part of the park's collection.
Event
A wild bat found at the San Diego Zoo Safari Park tested positive for rabies. This is a confirmed event reported by county health officials and Patch media.
Scale, impact and risk
This is the 19th bat to test positive for rabies in San Diego County this year (2026). In 2025, 21 bats tested positive. No human contact has been reported. The risk is considered high for individuals with direct contact, as rabies is nearly 100% fatal if untreated.
Response, status and uncertainty
County health officials are investigating to ensure anyone who came into contact with the bat receives care quickly. They have urged anyone who touched the bat to call County Public Health Services at 619-692-8499. No human cases have been reported yet. The source is a full article from Patch.

Rabies remains ‘threat’ in Pangnirtung with multiple dogs, fox infected - Nunatsiaq News
完整新闻中文摘要努纳武特地区政府在第四只疑似患有狂犬病的狗在庞纳通被杀后发出警告,自五月份以来,另外三只狗和一只狐狸也被确诊为狂犬病阳性。居民如果接触过散养的哈士奇,请立即寻求医疗评估,并报告任何表现出异常行为的动物。狂犬病仍然是社区中的一个活跃而严重的威胁。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该新闻报道发布于 2026 年 7 月 15 日美国东部时间上午 9:29。该事件涉及一只狗,该狗于 2026 年 7 月 11 日星期六出现狂犬病症状后被杀死,这也是自 2026 年 5 月以来第四只出现狂犬病症状的狗。
地点与对象
Pangnirtung,加拿大努纳武特地区的一个社区。受影响的人群包括可能在 2026 年 7 月 10 日星期五接触过一只松散的大型黑白哈士奇的居民,以及庞纳通 (Pangnirtung) 的一般社区。
事件
努纳武特地区政府已就庞纳唐地区活跃的狂犬病威胁发布公共服务公告和警告。在此之前,第四只疑似患有狂犬病的狗被杀,另外三只狗和一只狐狸此前已确诊患有狂犬病。
规模、影响与风险
自2026年5月以来,已有四只狗出现狂犬病症状,其中三只狗确诊呈阳性,一只狐狸确诊呈阳性。这种威胁被描述为“活跃且严重”。狂犬病是一种病毒性疾病,如果不及时治疗,几乎总是致命的。该风险包括人类通过接触受感染的动物而可能暴露的风险,特别是那些表现出异常行为的动物。
应对、状态与不确定性
第四只狗被杀死,但“无法”进行测试。卫生部敦促居民如果接触过这只散养的哈士奇犬,请立即进行医疗评估,并报告任何表现出狂犬病症状的动物。建议居民将狗拴起来并监测它们的症状。

Rabies remains ‘threat’ in Pangnirtung with multiple dogs, fox infected - Nunatsiaq News

(Photo courtesy of Wikimedia Commons/NIAID) Health Jul 15, 2026 – 9:29 am EDT Rabies remains ‘threat’ in Pangnirtung with multiple dogs, fox infected Nunavut government issues warning after fourth dog suspected to have disease is killed By Nunatsiaq News Anybody in Pangnirtung who had contact with a loose, large black and white husky last Friday should be seen by a nurse and assessed for rabies, says Nunavut’s Department of Health. The department issued a public service announcement Wednesday after the husky was killed Saturday for showing signs of the disease. The dog was “not available” to be tested, the public service announcement said. This is the fourth dog to show signs of rabies since May. The three other dogs tested positive for the disease, as has one fox. “Rabies remains an active and serious threat in the community,” the announcement said. Rabies is a viral disease that is almost always fatal if left untreated. Signs of rabies in animals includes strange or aggressive behaviour, staggering, frothing at the mouth, choking and making strange noises. “Wild animals infected with rabies may also appear friendly and approach humans without fear,” the announcement said. “This is abnormal behaviour for wild animals. Animals exhibiting these signs should be avoided and reported to the environmental health officer or the conservation officer.” Anybody who is bitten or scratched by an animal behaving this way should go to the health centre immediately, as treatment should be started quickly after exposure, the announcement said. The Department of Health is also urging people in Pangnirtung to keep their dogs tied up and to monitor them for signs of rabies. Pangnirtung, a community in Nunavut, Canada. The affected population includes residents who may have had contact with a loose, large black and white husky on Friday, July 10, 2026, and the general community of Pangnirtung.
Event
The Nunavut government has issued a public service announcement and warning regarding an active rabies threat in Pangnirtung. This follows the killing of a fourth dog suspected of having rabies, with three other dogs and one fox previously confirmed positive for the disease.
Scale, impact and risk
Since May 2026, four dogs have shown signs of rabies, with three confirmed positive and one fox confirmed positive. The threat is described as 'active and serious.' Rabies is a viral disease that is almost always fatal if left untreated. The risk includes potential human exposure through contact with infected animals, particularly those showing abnormal behavior.
Response, status and uncertainty
The fourth dog was killed but was 'not available' for testing. The Department of Health is urging residents to seek medical assessment if they had contact with the loose husky and to report any animals exhibiting signs of rabies. Residents are advised to keep dogs tied up and monitor them for symptoms.

Privacy Policy Accept Deny Non-Essential Lake George officer gets rabies shot after dead beaver scratch News Staff WNYT Updated: July 14, 2026 - 6:18 PM Published: July 14, 2026 - 12:46 PM Lake George (File / WNYT) LAKE GEORGE, N.Y. (WNYT) – A Lake George marine patrol officer got a preventive rabies shot after being scratched by a dead beaver that later tested negative, according to the Lake George Park Commission. The Lake George Park Commission said the scratch happened Friday, July 10, while a marine patrol officer was removing an already deceased beaver from Lake George near Glen Island. “Out of an abundance of caution, the officer received a rabies vaccination as a preventative against potential infection. The recovered animal ultimately tested negative for rabies,” the commission said. (An earlier version of this story wrongly stated the marine patrol officer was attacked by a rabid beaver. NewsChannel 13 apologizes for the error.) Scale, impact and risk
The scale of impact is limited to one individual. The risk of rabies infection was mitigated by the officer receiving a preventive vaccination. The beaver tested negative for rabies.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Lake George Park Commission confirmed the incident and the officer's vaccination. The beaver was tested and found negative for rabies. The source is a reputable media outlet (WNYT.com).

州政府提醒罗德岛居民狂犬病预防方法 - 补丁

State Reminds Rhode Island Residents About Rabies Prevention Methods - Patch
完整新闻中文摘要由于今年狂犬病动物数量增加,罗德岛州卫生官员向居民发出有关狂犬病预防的公众提醒。罗德岛州卫生部和环境管理部报告称,有 14 只浣熊和其他动物狂犬病检测呈阳性,超过了 2021 年至 2025 年期间 24 只的年平均水平。官员强调,狂犬病是一种致命的病毒性疾病,影响所有哺乳动物,但在接触狂犬病后通过及时医疗护理是可以预防的。该指南建议避免接触流浪动物或野生动物,保护好垃圾桶,并控制宠物以降低感染风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
新闻发布于2026-07-17。该事件涉及2026年春季和夏季罗德岛发现的狂犬病动物,2021年至2025年的年平均狂犬病动物数量为24只,这一数字已在2026年被超越。
地点与对象
受影响的人群包括所有罗德岛居民、他们的家人和他们的宠物。受影响的动物包括 14 只浣熊、七只蝙蝠、一只猫、一只土狼、一只臭鼬和一只土拨鼠。
事件
罗德岛州卫生部 (RIDOH) 和罗德岛州环境管理部 (DEM) 发布新闻稿,提醒居民注意狂犬病预防方法,以应对今年春季和夏季该州发现的狂犬病动物数量增加。
规模、影响与风险
2026 年罗德岛州患狂犬病的动物数量已超过 2021 年至 2025 年期间的年均 24 只。狂犬病是一种病毒性疾病,影响包括人类在内的所有哺乳动物,一旦出现症状几乎总是致命的,不过如果在接触后及时接受医疗护理,狂犬病是可以预防的。
应对、状态与不确定性
罗德岛州卫生部和罗德岛州环境管理部已发布狂犬病预防官方指南。该指南包括避免接触流浪或自由漫游的家养和野生动物、保护垃圾桶以及控制宠物的建议。来源是完整的原始报告正文。
B routine priority

State Reminds Rhode Island Residents About Rabies Prevention Methods - Patch

Fetched Original Body
Health & Fitness Rabid Animals In RI Prompt Officials To Share Prevention Methods More rabid animals have already turned up in Rhode Island this year than the national average, including 14 raccoons. More rabid animals have already turned up in Rhode Island this year than the annual average, including 14 raccoons, according to two state agencies. "In response to the increased number of rabid animals identified in Rhode Island this spring and summer, the Rhode Island Department of Health (RIDOH) and the Rhode Island Department of Environmental Management (DEM) are reminding all Rhode Islanders to take steps to protect themselves, their families, and their pets from rabies," the two agencies said in a media release. The yearly average of 24 rabid animals between 2021 and 2025 has already been eclipsed in 2026. In addition to the 14 raccoons, the rabid animals identified were seven bats, a cat, a coyote, a skunk and a woodchuck. Find out what's happening in Across Rhode Islandfor free with the latest updates from Patch. "Rabies is a viral disease that affects all mammals, including people, and is almost always fatal once symptoms develop," the release said. "However, it is preventable if medical care is received promptly after an exposure. The virus is most commonly transmitted through the bite of an infected animal, but it can also spread when saliva from a rabid animal enters the body through a scratch or an open wound." The department of health recommended the following rabies prevention methods: Find out what's happening in Across Rhode Islandfor free with the latest updates from Patch. - Avoid all contact with and do not feed stray or free-roaming domestic animals. - Avoid all contact with and do not feed wild animals. - Do not feed your animals outdoors, as this will attract other animals. This is especially dangerous when feeding large numbers of free-roaming cats. - Protect your pets by always maintaining control. Walk dogs on a leash or let them play in a fenced yard. Do not let pets wander unsupervised. - Securely cover all garbage cans so wild animals cannot scavenge for food. See also: Get more local news delivered straight to your inbox. Sign up for free Patch newsletters and alerts

Time
The news was published on 2026-07-17. The event involves rabid animals identified in Rhode Island during the spring and summer of 2026, with the yearly average of 24 rabid animals between 2021 and 2025 already being eclipsed in 2026.
Location and population
The affected population includes all Rhode Islanders, their families, and their pets. The affected animals include 14 raccoons, seven bats, a cat, a coyote, a skunk, and a woodchuck.
Event
The Rhode Island Department of Health (RIDOH) and the Rhode Island Department of Environmental Management (DEM) issued a media release to remind residents about rabies prevention methods in response to an increased number of rabid animals identified in the state this spring and summer.
Scale, impact and risk
The number of rabid animals in Rhode Island in 2026 has already exceeded the yearly average of 24 between 2021 and 2025. Rabies is a viral disease that affects all mammals, including people, and is almost always fatal once symptoms develop, though it is preventable if medical care is received promptly after an exposure.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Rhode Island Department of Health and the Rhode Island Department of Environmental Management have issued official guidance on rabies prevention. The guidance includes recommendations to avoid contact with stray or free-roaming domestic and wild animals, secure garbage cans, and keep pets under control. 应对、状态与不确定性
格林布赖尔县卫生部门发布了预防提示,并强调了狂犬病暴露后预防 (PEP) 的有效性。来源是部分摘录,因此未审查完整的原始文章。

Raccoon tests positive for rabies in Greenbrier County Updated Thu, July 2, 2026 at 7:29 PM UTC CRAWLEY, WV (WVNS) — A raccoon found in the Crawley area of Greenbrier County tested positive for rabies, according to the Greenbrier County Health Department . Both the CDC the Health Department have provided residents with tips and steps to protect their families, pets and homes. According to both organizations the three key steps to rabies prevention are as follows: Making sure your pets are up to date on their vaccinations Giving wild animals adequate space Securing your home of trash or anything else that could attract unwanted wildlife If bitten or scratched, immediate cleaning of the wound for at least 15 minutes is necessary. It is also important to seek immediate medical attention from a hospital or other healthcare provider. The Greenbrier County Health Department closed the statement by saying that rabies post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP) is “highly effective” if given as soon as possible. Third Lane County bat tests positive for rabies, officials warn - The Register-Guard

Fetched Original Body
HEALTHCARE Third Lane County bat tests positive for rabies, officials warn Alan Torres Eugene Register-Guard July 13, 2026, 3:39 p.m. PT Keep Watching A bat found in Lane County tested positive for rabies , and it's the third one so far this year, Lane County Public Health warned July 13. In the announcement, county and state officials urged members of the public to vaccinate their dogs and cats against rabies if they haven't already. "All pet owners should make certain their dogs and cats are vaccinated against rabies. When our pets are protected from rabies, it provides a buffer zone of immune animals between humans and rabid wild animals, such as bats," said Dr. Emilio DeBess, state public health veterinarian. Thirteen bats have tested positive for rabies in Oregon so far this year. Rabies is a viral disease that affects the nervous system of humans and mammals. It is "almost 100 percent" fatal once symptoms begin, the county said. The virus is carried in the saliva of an infected animal and can be transmitted when that animal bites, or in rarer instances, scratches another animal or person. If you or your pet is bitten by a bat, you should thoroughly clean the wound with soap and water, seek medical attention, report the event to Lane County Public Health and, if possible, capture the bat so it can be tested. There are several strains of rabies found in the U.S. but only bat rabies is found in Oregon. Other animals that have tested positive for rabies in Oregon contracted it from bats. According to Lane County, people should stay away from bats and not handle them. Alan Torres covers local government for the Register-Guard.
